\displaystyle\hat{E}_{0,2}

\displaystyle=1-24\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{q^{n}}{1-q^{n}}-\frac{3}{\pi\tau_{2}}\ ,

(C.12)

\displaystyle E_{1,2}

\displaystyle=\tau_{2}-\frac{45}{2\pi^{3}\tau_{2}^{\;2}}\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{n^{3}}\frac{1-q^{n}\bar{q}^{n}}{(1-q^{n})(1-\bar{q}^{n})}-\frac{180}{\pi}\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{n}\frac{q^{n}(1+q^{n})}{(1-q^{n})^{3}}-\frac{90}{\pi^{2}\tau_{2}}\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{n^{2}}\frac{q^{n}}{(1-q^{n})^{2}}

The Weierstrass function satisfies the identity

(\wp^{\prime})^{2}=4\wp^{3}-\frac{4}{3}\pi^{4}E_{4}(\tau)\wp-\frac{8}{27}\pi^{6}E_{6}(\tau)=4(\wp-e_{1})(\wp-e_{2})(\wp-e_{3})\;,

(C.13)

where
-e_{i}
are the values of the Weierstrass function at the half-periods

e_{1}=-\wp(\tfrac{1}{2})\quad,\quad e_{2}=-\wp(\tfrac{1+\tau}{2})\quad,\quad e_{2}=-\wp(\tfrac{\tau}{2})\;.

(C.14)

The functions
e_{i}
are related to the Jacobi functions by the identity

e_{i}(\tau)=4\pi i\partial_{\tau}\log\frac{\vartheta_{i+1}(0|\tau)}{\eta(\tau)}\;,

(C.15)

and satisfy

e_{i}^{2}=-i\pi\partial_{\tau}e_{i}+4\pi ie_{i}\partial_{\tau}\log\eta+\frac{2\pi^{4}}{9}E_{4}(\tau)\;.

(C.16)

Deriving (
C.13
) with respect to
z
we can write the second derivative of
\wp
in terms of
\wp
itself

\wp^{\prime\prime}(z)=6\wp^{2}(z)-\frac{2\pi^{4}}{3}E_{4}(\tau)\;.

(C.17)

We will also use the following relation between Weierstrass and Jacobi functions

[
99
]

\wp(z_{1})-\wp(z_{2})=4\pi^{2}\eta(\tau)^{6}\frac{\vartheta_{1}(z_{1}+z_{2})\vartheta_{1}(z_{2}-z_{1})}{\vartheta_{1}(z_{1})^{2}\vartheta_{1}(z_{2})^{2}}\;.

(C.18)

For the purpose of computing the helicity supertraces, it is also useful to express derivatives of
\vartheta_{1}
in terms of the first derivative of
\vartheta_{1}
and the Weierstrass function using (
C.7
)

\displaystyle\vartheta_{1}^{\prime\prime}(z)

\displaystyle=\frac{\vartheta_{1}^{\prime}(z)^{2}}{\vartheta_{1}(z)}+\frac{\vartheta_{1}^{(3)}(0)}{3\vartheta_{1}^{\prime}(0)}\vartheta_{1}(z)-\vartheta_{1}(z){\wp(z)}

(C.19)

\displaystyle\vartheta_{1}^{(3)}(z)

\displaystyle=\frac{\vartheta_{1}^{\prime}(z)^{3}}{\vartheta_{1}(z)^{2}}+\frac{\vartheta_{1}^{(3)}(0)}{\vartheta_{1}^{\prime}(0)}\vartheta_{1}^{\prime}(z)-\vartheta_{1}(z)\wp^{\prime}(z)-3{\wp(z)}\vartheta_{1}^{\prime}(z)

(C.20)

\displaystyle\vartheta_{1}^{(4)}(z)

\displaystyle=\frac{\vartheta_{1}^{\prime}(z)^{4}}{\vartheta_{1}(z)^{3}}+\frac{2\vartheta_{1}^{(3)}(0)}{\vartheta_{1}^{\prime}(0)}\frac{\vartheta_{1}^{\prime}(z)^{2}}{\vartheta_{1}(z)}+\frac{\vartheta_{1}^{(3)}(0)^{2}}{3\vartheta_{1}^{\prime}(0)^{2}}\vartheta_{1}(z)-\vartheta_{1}(z){\wp}^{\prime\prime}(z)-4\vartheta_{1}^{\prime}(z)\wp^{\prime}(z)

\displaystyle~~-\frac{6{\wp(z)}\vartheta_{1}^{\prime}(z)^{2}}{\vartheta_{1}(z)}-\frac{2\vartheta_{1}^{(3)}(0)}{\vartheta_{1}^{\prime}(0)}\vartheta_{1}(z){\wp(z)}+3\vartheta_{1}(z){\wp(z)}^{2}

(C.21)

and also

\vartheta_{1}^{\prime}(0)=2\pi\eta^{3}\quad,\quad\vartheta_{1}^{(3)}(0)=-\pi^{3}E_{2}\eta^{3}\quad,\quad\vartheta_{1}^{(5)}(0)=\frac{10}{3}\pi^{5}E_{2}^{2}\eta^{3}-\frac{4}{3}\pi^{5}E_{4}\eta^{3}

(C.22)

In Section
2
we define

\xi(v)=\frac{\sin\pi v}{\pi}\frac{\vartheta_{1}^{\prime}(0)}{\vartheta_{1}(v)}

(C.23)

which is an even function satisfying
\xi(0)=1
. One shows that its first two non-vanishing derivatives give

\displaystyle\xi^{\prime\prime}(0)=

\displaystyle\,-\frac{1}{3}\left[\frac{\vartheta_{1}^{\prime\prime\prime}(0)}{\vartheta_{1}^{\prime}(0)}+\pi^{2}\right]=\frac{\pi^{2}}{3}(E_{2}-1)\;,

(C.24)

\displaystyle\xi^{(4)}(0)

\displaystyle\,=\frac{\pi^{4}}{5}+\frac{2\pi^{2}}{3}\frac{\vartheta_{1}^{\prime\prime\prime}(0)}{\vartheta_{1}^{\prime}(0)}{+}\frac{2}{3}\Bigg[\frac{\vartheta_{1}^{\prime\prime\prime}(0)}{\vartheta_{1}^{\prime}(0)}\Bigg]^{2}{-}\frac{1}{5}\frac{\vartheta_{1}^{(5)}(0)}{\vartheta_{1}^{\prime}(0)}={\pi^{4}\over 15}(3-10E_{2}+2E_{4}+5E_{2}^{2})\;.

(C.25)

Let us now give some formulae about the torus propagator, defined as

{{G}}(z_{1},z_{2}|\tau)={{G}}(z_{12}|\tau)=-\log\left|\frac{\vartheta_{1}(z_{12}|\tau)}{\vartheta_{1}^{\prime}(0|\tau)}\right|^{2}+2\pi\frac{{\rm Im}^{2}z_{12}}{{\rm Im}\tau}\;.

(C.26)

It will be convenient to consider the Green function without zero mode as in
[
98
]

\mathcal{G}(z|\tau)=G(z|\tau)-2\log 2\pi|\eta(\tau)|^{2}=-\log\left|\frac{\vartheta_{1}(z|\tau)}{\eta(\tau)}\right|^{2}+2\pi\frac{{\rm Im}^{2}z}{{\rm Im}\tau}\;,

(C.27)

which admits the regularised Fourier expansion

{{{\mathcal{G}}}}_{\epsilon}(z|\tau)=\frac{1}{\pi}\left.\sum_{m,n}\right.^{\prime}\frac{\tau_{2}^{1+\epsilon}}{|m\tau-n|^{2+2\epsilon}}e^{2\pi i(mx+ny)}\;,

(C.28)

such that
{{{\mathcal{G}}}}(z|\tau)=\lim_{\epsilon\rightarrow 0}{{{\mathcal{G}}}}_{\epsilon}(z|\tau)
.
We will often refer to the torus propagator as the one without the zero mode, since the zero mode drops out from the Koba–Nielsen factor by momentum conservation.

In Section
4
we also mention the annulus propagator
G_{A}
, which is defined from the torus propagator as

{{G}}_{A}(z_{1},z_{2}|\tau_{A})=\frac{1}{2}[{G}(z_{1},z_{2}|\tau_{A}){+}{G}(z_{1},1{-}\bar{z}_{2}|\tau_{A}){+}{G}(1{-}\bar{z}_{1},z_{2}|\tau_{A}){+}{G}(1{-}\bar{z}_{1},1{-}\bar{z}_{2}|\tau_{A})]

(C.29)

where
\tau_{A}=iT/2
and it reduces to

{{G}}_{A}(z_{1},z_{2}|\tau_{A})=-4\log\frac{\vartheta_{1}(z_{12}|\tau)}{\vartheta_{1}^{\prime}(0|\tau)}+4\pi\frac{{\rm Im}^{2}z_{12}}{{\rm Im}\tau}

(C.30)

where we used that the annulus can be obtained from the torus from the involution
\tilde{z}=1{-}z
.

In the one-loop amplitude we meet the function
{\mathcal{Y}}(z|\tau)
defined as

\displaystyle{\mathcal{Y}}(z|\tau)

\displaystyle=-2[\wp(z|\tau)-(\partial_{z}{{{\mathcal{G}}}})^{2}(z|\tau)]

(C.31)

\displaystyle=2(\partial_{z}{{{\mathcal{G}}}})^{2}(z|\tau)-2\partial_{z}^{2}{{{\mathcal{G}}}}(z|\tau)-4\pi i\partial_{\tau}\log[\tau_{2}\,\eta^{2}(\tau)]

where in the latter equation we used the identity

\wp(z|\tau)-\partial_{z}^{2}{{{\mathcal{G}}}}(z|\tau)=4\pi i\partial_{\tau}\log[\eta(\tau)\sqrt{\tau_{2}}]\;.

(C.32)

Using the definition of the torus propagator and the heat equation for
\vartheta_{1}
, the function
{\mathcal{Y}}
can be written in a form in which the absence of double pole is manifest

{\mathcal{Y}}(z|\tau)=8\pi i\left[\partial_{\tau}\log\frac{\vartheta_{1}(z|\tau)}{\eta(\tau)}+\frac{{\rm Im}z}{\tau_{2}}\partial_{z}\log\vartheta_{1}(z|\tau)+i\pi\frac{{\rm Im}^{2}z}{\tau_{2}^{2}}\right]

(C.33)

since the terms
[\vartheta_{1}^{\prime}(z)]^{2}
, which should yield the double pole, cancel.

One defines the Szegö kernel as

\displaystyle S_{\alpha}(z_{1},z_{2};\tau)=S_{\alpha}(z_{12})=\begin{cases}S(z_{12})=-\partial_{z_{1}}\mathcal{G}(z_{12})&\text{if $\alpha=1$,}\\
\dfrac{\vartheta_{\alpha}(z_{12})\vartheta_{1}^{\prime}(0)}{\vartheta_{1}(z_{12})\vartheta_{\alpha}(0)}&\text{if $\alpha>1$.}\end{cases}

(C.34)

When
\alpha>1
the Szegö kernel satisfies the identities

\displaystyle S_{i+1}^{2}(z)

\displaystyle=\wp(z)+e_{i}

(C.35)

\displaystyle S_{i+1}(z_{12})S_{i+1}(z_{23})

\displaystyle=S_{i+1}(z_{13})\,\omega_{123}-\partial_{z_{1}}S_{i+1}(z_{13})

(C.36)

where

\omega_{123}=S(z_{12})+S(z_{23})+S(z_{31})=\partial_{z_{1}}\log\vartheta_{1}(z_{12}){+}\partial_{z_{2}}\log\vartheta_{1}(z_{23}){+}\partial_{z_{3}}\log\vartheta_{1}(z_{31})\;.

(C.37)

A variant of Fay’s trisecant identity gives

\Omega_{123}\equiv S(z_{12})S(z_{23})+S(z_{23})S(z_{31})+S(z_{31})S(z_{12})=-\frac{1}{4}\left(\mathcal{Y}(z_{12})+\mathcal{Y}(z_{23})+\mathcal{Y}(z_{31})\right)\;.

(C.38)

Appendix D
Details on the 1-loop open string amplitude

In this appendix with simplify the one-loop correlators (
4.18
),(
4.19
),(
4.20
) for a choice of polarisations
[--++]
such that

a^{-}_{1}\cdot a^{-}_{2}=a^{+}_{3}\cdot a^{+}_{4}=k_{2}\cdot a_{3}^{+}=k_{2}\cdot a_{4}^{+}=k_{3}\cdot a_{1}^{-}=k_{3}\cdot a_{2}^{-}=0\;.

(D.1)

One can then simplify
\mathbb{G}^{L}_{\textup{2-bil}}[^{r}_{s}]
in (
4.20
) as

a_{1}{\cdot}{\cal P}_{1}\,a_{2}{\cdot}{\cal P}_{2}=\frac{1}{4}(f_{1}f_{2})(2S_{12}^{2}+\Omega_{123}+\Omega_{412})\;.

(D.2)

We then compute the field strength traces using spinor-helicity formalism, obtaining that the only non vanishing traces of
f
’s are

(f_{1}f_{2}f_{3}f_{4})=(f_{1}f_{3}f_{2}f_{4})=(f_{1}f_{3}f_{4}f_{2})=\frac{1}{4}(f_{1}f_{2})(f_{3}f_{4})=\frac{1}{4}\langle 12\rangle^{2}[34]^{2}\;.

(D.3)

\mathbb{G}^{L}_{\textup{4-bil,conn}}[^{r}_{s}]
in (
4.18
) can be simplified using the Fay trisecant identity (
C.38
) and

\omega_{123}\omega_{341}+\omega_{124}\omega_{431}+\omega_{132}\omega_{241}+\omega_{234}\omega_{412}+\omega_{243}\omega_{312}+\omega_{324}\omega_{413}=-\sum_{i<j}S_{ij}^{2}-\sum_{i}\Omega_{i}\;,

(D.4)

where we introduced for short
\Omega_{i}=\Omega_{i+1,i+2,i+3}
. Finally we can write almost all the contributions in terms of
\mathcal{Y}
functions

\displaystyle\mathbb{G}^{L}_{\textup{4-bil,conn}}[^{r}_{s}]

\displaystyle=-3\langle 12\rangle^{2}[34]^{2}\mathcal{F}[^{r}_{s}]

(D.5)

\displaystyle\mathbb{G}^{L}_{\textup{4-bil,disc}}[^{r}_{s}]

\displaystyle=\langle 12\rangle^{2}[34]^{2}\left[\mathcal{F}[^{r}_{s}]-\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{E}[^{r}_{s}](\wp_{12}+\wp_{34})\right]

(D.6)

\displaystyle\mathbb{G}^{L}_{\textup{2-bil}}[^{r}_{s}]

\displaystyle=\langle 12\rangle^{2}[34]^{2}\mathcal{E}[^{r}_{s}]\left(\frac{1}{2}S_{12}^{2}+\frac{1}{2}S_{34}^{2}-\frac{1}{8}\sum_{i<j}\mathcal{Y}_{ij}\right)

(D.7)

Combining the three contributions the double poles cancel and using that
\mathcal{F}[^{r}_{s}]=-\tfrac{1}{2}\wp(u_{r,s})\mathcal{E}[^{r}_{s}]
, we finally get (
4.22
).

Appendix E
Elliptic integrals

In this section we compute the relevant integrals that appear in Section
4
.

E.1
Few simple integrals

We start with vanishing integrals. Using the Fourier expansion of the propagator (
C.28
) it is straightforward to prove that the integral of
{{{\mathcal{G}}}}
and its derivatives vanish

\int\frac{d^{2}z}{\tau_{2}}\,{{{\mathcal{G}}}}(z|\tau)=0\quad,\quad\int\frac{d^{2}z}{\tau_{2}}\,\partial_{z}^{k}{{{\mathcal{G}}}}(z|\tau)=0\;.

(E.1)

One can also prove that the integral of
{\mathcal{Y}}
vanishes using (
C.31
). The integral of
\wp
can be computed using the identity (
C.32
)

\int\frac{d^{2}z}{\tau_{2}}\,\wp(z|\tau)=4\pi i\,\partial_{\tau}\log[\eta(\tau)\sqrt{\tau_{2}}]=-\frac{\pi^{2}}{6}\hat{E}_{2}(\tau)\;.

(E.2)

The integral of
(\partial_{z}{{{\mathcal{G}}}})^{2}
can be performed using the Fourier expansion (
C.28
)

\displaystyle\int\frac{d^{2}z}{\tau_{2}}\,(\partial_{z}{{{\mathcal{G}}}}_{\epsilon})^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left.\sum_{m,n}\right.^{\prime}\left.\sum_{m^{\prime},n^{\prime}}\right.^{\prime}\int_{0}^{1}dx\,\int_{0}^{1}dy\,\frac{e^{2\pi i[(m+m^{\prime})x+(n+n^{\prime})y]}\,\tau_{2}^{2\epsilon}}{(m\tau{-}n)(m^{\prime}\tau{-}n^{\prime})|m\tau{-}n|^{2\epsilon}|m^{\prime}\tau{-}n^{\prime}|^{2\epsilon}}

(E.3)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\tau_{2}^{2\epsilon}\left.\sum_{m,n}\right.^{\prime}\frac{1}{(m\tau{-}n)^{2}|m\tau{-}n|^{4\epsilon}}=-2\zeta(2+4\epsilon)\,E_{2\epsilon,2}(\tau)

\displaystyle\underset{\epsilon\rightarrow 0}{=}

\displaystyle-\frac{\pi^{2}}{3}\,\hat{E}_{2}(\tau)\;.

Combining these results with (
C.31
) we get

\int\frac{d^{2}z}{\tau_{2}}\,{\mathcal{Y}}(z|\tau)=0\;.

(E.4)

E.2
The integral of
{{{\mathcal{G}}}}(z){\mathcal{Y}}(z)

In this section we compute the integral (
4.31
)

{\mathcal{I}}(\tau)\equiv\int\frac{d^{2}z}{\tau_{2}}\,{{{\mathcal{G}}}}(z){\mathcal{Y}}(z)\;.

(E.5)

Using (
C.33
) one finds that
{\mathcal{I}}(\tau)
converges, and it will be convenient to use

\displaystyle{\mathcal{I}}(\tau)

\displaystyle=\int\frac{d^{2}z}{\tau_{2}}\,{{{\mathcal{G}}}}(z)\,\left[\,-8\pi^{2}\frac{{\rm Im}^{2}z}{\tau_{2}^{2}}+8\pi i\partial_{\tau}\log\frac{\vartheta_{1}(z)}{\eta}+8\pi i\frac{{\rm Im}z}{\tau_{2}}\partial_{z}\log\vartheta_{1}(z)\,\right]\;,

(E.6)

which is manifestly free of double pole divergence.
We then write
\partial_{z}\log\vartheta_{1}
and
\partial_{\tau}\log\vartheta_{1}/\eta
as
q
-expansions

\displaystyle\partial_{z}\log\vartheta_{1}(z|\tau)

\displaystyle=-i\pi-\frac{2\pi ie^{2\pi iz}}{1-e^{2\pi iz}}-2\pi i\sum_{\ell=1}^{\infty}\frac{q^{\ell}}{1-q^{\ell}}\left(e^{2\pi i\ell z}-e^{-2\pi i\ell z}\right)

(E.7)

\displaystyle\partial_{\tau}\log\frac{\vartheta_{1}(z|\tau)}{\eta(\tau)}

\displaystyle=\frac{\pi i}{6}-2\pi i\sum_{\ell=1}^{\infty}\frac{q^{\ell}}{(1-q^{\ell})^{2}}(e^{2\pi iz\ell}+e^{-2\pi iz\ell})

(E.8)

Considering that the integral of
{{{\mathcal{G}}}}
vanishes and also that

\int\frac{d^{2}z}{\tau_{2}}{{{\mathcal{G}}}}(z){\rm Im}(z)=\int\frac{d^{2}z}{\tau_{2}}{{{\mathcal{G}}}}(1+\tau-z){\rm Im}(1+\tau-z)=-\int\frac{d^{2}z}{\tau_{2}}{{{\mathcal{G}}}}(z){\rm Im}(z)=0

(E.9)

when we insert the expansions (
E.7
) and (
E.8
) in (
E.6
) we get

\displaystyle{\mathcal{I}}(\tau)

\displaystyle=16\pi^{2}\!\!\int\!dx\,dy\,{{{\mathcal{G}}}}(x,y)\Bigg\{\sum_{\ell=1}^{\infty}\frac{q^{\ell}}{(1{-}q^{\ell})^{2}}(e^{2\pi iz\ell}{+}e^{-2\pi iz\ell})+\sum_{\ell=1}^{\infty}\frac{yq^{\ell}}{1{-}q^{\ell}}(e^{2\pi i\ell z}{-}e^{-2\pi i\ell z})+

(E.10)

\displaystyle+\frac{y\,e^{2\pi iz}}{1{-}e^{2\pi iz}}-\frac{y^{2}}{2}\Bigg\}=16\pi^{2}[{\mathcal{I}}^{(1)}(\tau)+{\mathcal{I}}^{(2)}(\tau)+{\mathcal{I}}^{(3)}(\tau)+{\mathcal{I}}^{(4)}(\tau)]\;.

We compute the contributions
{\mathcal{I}}^{(i)}
separately using the Fourier expansion of the propagator in (
C.28
), where we set
\epsilon=0
directly because all the integrals converge. We first compute the following integrals

\displaystyle\int\frac{d^{2}z}{\tau_{2}}{{{\mathcal{G}}}}(z)e^{2\pi iz\ell}

\displaystyle=\frac{\tau_{2}}{2\pi^{2}i}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\frac{q^{\ell}-1}{|n+\ell\tau|^{2}(n+\ell\tau)}

(E.11)

\displaystyle\int\frac{d^{2}z}{\tau_{2}}{{{\mathcal{G}}}}(z)\,y\,e^{2\pi iz\ell}

\displaystyle=\frac{\tau_{2}}{2\pi^{2}i}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\left[\frac{q^{\ell}}{|n+\ell\tau|^{2}(n+\ell\tau)}+\frac{1-q^{\ell}}{2\pi i|n+\ell\tau|^{2}(n+\ell\tau)^{2}}\right]

Using the above formulae we can easily compute
{\mathcal{I}}^{(1)}(\tau)
and
{\mathcal{I}}^{(2)}(\tau)
obtaining

\displaystyle{\mathcal{I}}^{(1)}(\tau)+{\mathcal{I}}^{(2)}(\tau)=\frac{\tau_{2}}{2\pi^{2}i}\sum_{\ell=1}^{\infty}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\left[-\frac{q^{\ell}}{|n+\ell\tau|^{2}(n+\ell\tau)}+\frac{1+q^{\ell}}{2\pi i|n+\ell\tau|^{2}(n+\ell\tau)^{2}}\right]\;.

(E.12)

The third term can be computed similarly to (
E.11
) expanding the denominator
1-e^{2\pi iz}

\displaystyle{\mathcal{I}}^{(3)}(\tau)

\displaystyle=\frac{\tau_{2}}{2\pi^{2}i}\sum_{\ell=1}^{\infty}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\left[\frac{q^{\ell}}{|n+\ell\tau|^{2}(n+\ell\tau)}+\frac{1-q^{\ell}}{2\pi i|n+\ell\tau|^{2}(n+\ell\tau)^{2}}\right]\;.

(E.13)

The last integral reads

\displaystyle{\mathcal{I}}^{(4)}(\tau)

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\int dx\,dy\,{{{\mathcal{G}}}}(x,y)\,y^{2}=\frac{\tau_{2}}{\pi(2\pi i)^{2}}\sum_{n\neq 0}\frac{1}{|n+0\tau|^{2}(n+0\tau)^{2}}\;.

(E.14)

Combining all the
{\mathcal{I}}^{(i)}
we get an Eisenstein series

\sum_{i=1}^{4}{\mathcal{I}}^{(i)}(\tau)=-\frac{1}{4\pi^{3}}\left.\sum_{\ell,n}\right.^{\prime}\frac{\tau_{2}}{|n+\ell\tau|^{2}(n+\ell\tau)^{2}}=-\frac{\pi}{180}E_{1,2}(\tau)

(E.15)

and finally the result

{\mathcal{I}}(\tau)=-\frac{4\pi^{3}}{45}\,E_{1,2}(\tau)\;.

(E.16)

Appendix F
Lattices and Eisenstein series

In this appendix we shall derive some properties of the
SL(2,\mathds{H})
and
Sp(4,\mathds{H})
real analytic Eisenstein series that appear as one-loop couplings defined as Siegel–Narain theta lifts. The discrete groups and their congruent subgroups are identified as automorphism groups of even lattices. For simplicity we consider only the Hurwitz quaternions, but the formulae extend straightforwardly to the Eisenstein quaternions
\mathds{H}(3)
. All computations and proofs are indeed valid for
K=3
. In this section we write
R
for the radius
R_{\scalebox{0.6}{s}}
or
\tilde{R_{\scalebox{0.6}{s}}}
in string units for short.

F.1
SL(2,\mathds{H})
as
O(I\hskip-2.84526ptI_{1,1}\oplus D_{4})

A pseudo-real Weyl spinor of
Spin(1,5)
, or equivalently a real
SU(2)
doublet of Weyl spinor of
Spin(1,5)
, can be realised as a doublet of quaternions through the homomorphism
Spin(1,5)=SU^{*}(4)=SL(2,\mathds{H}(\mathds{R}))
. A vector of
SO(1,5)
is then a Hermitian two by two matrix over the quaternions.

The even lattice
I\hskip-2.84526ptI_{1,1}\oplus D_{4}
can be realised in this way as the set the Hermitian two by two matrix over the Hurwitz quaternions

Q=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}m&\,q\\
q^{*}&n\end{array}\right)\in\mathds{M}_{2}^{0}\qquad\mbox{with}\;n\in\mathds{Z}\,,\;m\in\mathds{Z}\,,\;q\in\mathds{H}\;.

(F.1)

The even bilinear form is
(Q,Q)=2\det Q=2mn-2|q|^{2}
. The automorphism group of
\mathds{M}_{2}^{0}=I\hskip-2.84526ptI_{1,1}\oplus D_{4}
is
SL(2,\mathds{H})/\mathds{Z}_{2}
, with the transformation

Q\rightarrow\gamma^{\dagger}Q\gamma\;.

(F.2)

Any vector
Q
of vanishing norm factorises in two spinors as
Q={\bf q}{\bf q}^{\dagger}
for a two-vector
{\bf q}
of Hurwitz quaternions. One can always find
\gamma\in SL(2,\mathds{H})
for
\det Q=0
such that

\gamma Q=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}{\rm gcd}(Q)&\,0\\
0&0\end{array}\right)

(F.3)

and
\gamma{\bf q}=({\rm gcrd}({\bf q}),0)
with
|{\rm gcrd}({\bf q})|^{2}={\rm gcd}(Q)
. The property of factorisation induced by norm factorisation summarised in (
A
) implies that for a given
{\rm gcd}(Q)\in\mathds{N}
there are

24\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}d|{\rm gcd}(Q)\\
2/\hskip-3.41432pt|\hskip 1.70717ptd\end{subarray}}d

(F.4)

distinct
{\rm gcrd}({\bf q})\in\mathds{H}
such that
|{\rm gcrd}({\bf q})|^{2}={\rm gcd}(Q)
. For a homogeneous function of degree
-s
,
f(\lambda Q)=\lambda^{-s}f(Q)
, one has therefore the factorisation

\sum^{\prime}_{{\bf q}\in\mathds{H}^{2}}f({\bf q}{\bf q}^{\dagger})=\sum^{\prime}_{\begin{subarray}{c}Q\in\mathds{M}_{2}^{0}\\
\det Q=0\end{subarray}}\Biggl(24\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}d|{\rm gcd}(Q)\\
2/\hskip-3.41432pt|\hskip 1.70717ptd\end{subarray}}d\Biggr)f(Q)=\frac{\zeta_{\mathds{H}}(s)}{2\zeta(s)}\sum^{\prime}_{\begin{subarray}{c}Q\in\mathds{M}_{2}^{0}\\
\det Q=0\end{subarray}}f(Q)

(F.5)

where
\zeta_{\mathds{H}}(s)
is the zeta function over the Hurwitz quaternions (
A.6
). It is convenient to introduce complete zeta functions satisfying functional relations. For the Riemann zeta function one one the standard

\xi(s)=\pi^{-\frac{s}{2}}\Gamma(\tfrac{s}{2})\zeta(s)\;,

(F.6)

and we introduce the completed zeta function over the Hurwitz quaternions as

\xi_{\mathds{H}}(s)=(\sqrt{2}\pi)^{-s}\Gamma(s)\zeta_{\mathds{H}}(s)\ .

(F.7)

It satisfies the functional identity
\xi_{\mathds{H}}(2-s)=\xi_{\mathds{H}}(s)
and is a meromorphic function of
s
with simple poles at
s=0,2
of residues
-1
and
1
. Its zero’s are located on the two lines Re
[s]=\frac{1}{2}
and Re
[s]=\frac{3}{2}
according to Riemann hypothesis.
41
41

41

There is the generalisation
\xi_{\mathds{H}(K)}(s)=\bigl(\tfrac{K}{4}\bigr)^{\frac{s}{2}}\pi^{-s}\Gamma(s)\zeta_{\mathds{H}(K)}(s)
.

The perturbative lattice is
I\hskip-2.84526ptI_{1,1}[2]\oplus D_{4}
, it is defined by the set of Hermitian two by two matrices over the Hurwitz quaternions with

Q=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}m&\,q\\
q^{*}&n\end{array}\right)\in\mathds{M}^{1}_{2}\qquad\mbox{with}\;n\in\mathds{Z}\,,\;m\in 2\mathds{Z}\,,\;q\in\mathds{H}\;,

(F.8)

that we write
\mathds{M}_{2}
or
\mathds{M}_{2}(2)
in the main text. We also introduce notations for the dual lattices

Q=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}m&\,q\\
q^{*}&n\end{array}\right)\in\mathds{M}^{2}_{2}=2\mathds{M}^{1*}_{2}\qquad\mbox{with}\;n\in\mathds{Z}\,,\;m\in 2\mathds{Z}\,,\;q\in\alpha\mathds{H}\;,

(F.9)

Q=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}m&\,q\\
q^{*}&n\end{array}\right)\in\mathds{M}^{3}_{2}=2\mathds{M}^{*}_{2}\qquad\mbox{with}\;n\in 2\mathds{Z}\,,\;m\in 2\mathds{Z}\,,\;q\in\alpha\mathds{H}\;,

(F.10)

with
\alpha
any Hurwitz quaternion of norm square
2
, e.g.
\alpha=1+e_{1}
.
Checking the representation on
\mathds{M}^{1}_{2}
, one finds that the congruent subgroup of
\Gamma^{\scalebox{0.6}{A}_{\scalebox{0.5}{3}}}_{0}(\alpha)\subset SL(2,\mathds{H})
preserving
I\hskip-2.84526ptI_{1,1}[2]\oplus D_{4}
is defined as

\gamma=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}a&\,b\\
c&\,d\end{array}\right)\in SL(2,\mathds{H})\;,\qquad c=0\;\mbox{mod}\,\alpha\;.

(F.11)

As explained in Appendix A, this condition is consistent with the group multiplication because
\alpha\mathds{H}
is a two-sided ideal and both
bc
and
ca
are in
\alpha\mathds{H}
. The automorphism group of the lattice
I\hskip-2.84526ptI_{1,1}[2]\oplus D_{4}
is in fact the conjugate
SL(2,\mathds{H})
group

\gamma=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}a&\,b\\
c&\,d\end{array}\right)\in\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}1&\,0\\
0&\,\alpha\end{array}\right)SL(2,\mathds{H})\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}1&\,0\\
0&\,\frac{1}{\alpha}\end{array}\right)\;,\qquad c\in\alpha\mathds{H}\,,\quad b\in\frac{1}{\alpha}\mathds{H}\;.

(F.12)

We write this group
\Gamma^{\scalebox{0.6}{A}_{\scalebox{0.5}{3}}}_{0*}(\alpha)
, as the group generated by
\Gamma^{\scalebox{0.6}{A}_{\scalebox{0.5}{3}}}_{0}(\alpha)
and the element

\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}0&-\frac{1}{\alpha}\\
\alpha&\,0\end{array}\right)

.

The integral spinors of
\Gamma^{\scalebox{0.6}{A}_{\scalebox{0.5}{3}}}_{0}(\alpha)
are the vectors in
\alpha\mathds{H}\oplus\mathds{H}
, and (
F.5
) generalises to

\sum^{\prime}_{{\bf q}\in\alpha\mathds{H}\oplus\mathds{H}}f({\bf q}{\bf q}^{\dagger})=\frac{\zeta_{\mathds{H}}(s)}{2\zeta(s)}\sum^{\prime}_{\begin{subarray}{c}Q\in\mathds{M}^{1}_{2}\\
\det Q=0\end{subarray}}f(Q)\;.

(F.13)

F.2
SL(2,\mathds{H})
Eisenstein series

One defines the
SL(2,\mathds{H})
Eisenstein series for
{\rm Re}[s]>4
as the absolutely convergent sums

E_{\alpha\mathds{H}\oplus\mathds{H},s}^{\mathfrak{sl}_{2}\mathds{H}}=\frac{1}{\zeta_{\mathds{H}}(s)}\sum_{{\bf q}\in\alpha\mathds{H}\oplus\mathds{H}}^{\prime}|v^{-1\dagger}({\bf q})|^{-2s}\;,\qquad E_{\mathds{H}^{2},s}^{\mathfrak{sl}_{2}\mathds{H}}=\frac{1}{\zeta_{\mathds{H}}(s)}\sum_{{\bf q}\in\mathds{H}\oplus\mathds{H}}^{\prime}|v^{-1\dagger}({\bf q})|^{-2s}\ ,

(F.14)

with the zeta function over the the Hurwitz quaternions (
A.6
). By construction
E_{\mathds{H}^{2},s}^{\mathfrak{sl}_{2}\mathds{H}}
is invariant under
SL(2,\mathds{H})
, while
E_{\alpha\mathds{H}\oplus\mathds{H},s}^{\mathfrak{sl}_{2}\mathds{H}}
is invariant under the congruent subgroup
\Gamma^{\scalebox{0.6}{A}_{\scalebox{0.5}{3}}}_{0}(\alpha)
. The Eisenstein series have analytic continuations to meromorphic functions of
s\in\mathds{C}
, with poles at
s\neq 4
and a countable set of points on the lines
{\rm Re}[s]=\frac{1}{2}
and
\frac{3}{2}
corresponding to the non-trivial zeros of the Riemann zeta function. They are normalised such that
E_{\alpha\mathds{H}\oplus\mathds{H},0}^{\mathfrak{sl}_{2}\mathds{H}}=E_{\mathds{H}^{2},0}^{\mathfrak{sl}_{2}\mathds{H}}=1
.

Writing

|v^{-1\dagger}({\bf q})|^{2}=R^{-1}|q+ap|^{2}+R|p|^{2}\;,

(F.15)

for
R\in\mathds{R}^{+}
and
a\in\mathds{H}(R)
, one finds the relation
42
42

42

This is the same analogy as for the Eisenstein series
E_{s,w}(\frac{\tau}{2})
for the sum over
m\in 2\mathds{Z}
.

E_{\alpha\mathds{H}\oplus\mathds{H},s}^{\mathfrak{sl}_{2}\mathds{H}}(R^{2},a)=2^{-\frac{s}{2}}E_{\mathds{H}^{2},s}^{\mathfrak{sl}_{2}\mathds{H}}(R^{2}/2,\alpha^{-1}a)\;.

(F.16)

Using Poisson summation formula one computes that

E_{\alpha\mathds{H}\oplus\mathds{H},s}^{\mathfrak{sl}_{2}\mathds{H}}=2^{-s}R^{s}+\frac{\xi_{\mathds{H}}(s-2)}{4\xi_{\mathds{H}}(s)}R^{4-s}\\
+2^{-\frac{s}{2}}\frac{24R^{2}}{\xi_{\mathds{H}}(s)}\sum_{q\in\mathds{H}}^{\prime}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}d|q\\
d=1\,{\rm mod}\,2\end{subarray}}\Bigl(\frac{d^{s-1}}{|q|^{s-2}}\sigma_{s-2}(\tfrac{|q|^{2}}{d^{2}})\Bigr)K_{s-2}(2\pi R|q|)e^{i\pi(q^{*}a+a^{*}q)}

(F.17)

where one uses (
A.13
). In this form the analytic continuation is manifest and this asymptotic expansion is absolutely convergent for almost all
s
.
It satisfies that
2^{\frac{s}{2}}\xi_{\mathds{H}}(s)E_{\alpha\mathds{H}\oplus\mathds{H},s}^{\mathfrak{sl}_{2}\mathds{H}}
is symmetric under
s\rightarrow 4-s
.

The same function is obtained by the Siegel–Narain theta series

\int_{\mathcal{F}_{2}}\frac{d^{2}\tau}{\tau_{2}^{\,2}}E_{\frac{s-3}{2},2}(\tau)\Gamma_{I\hskip-2.84526ptI_{1,1}[2]\oplus D_{4}}=(2-2^{s})\xi(s)E_{\alpha\mathds{H}\oplus\mathds{H},s}^{\mathfrak{sl}_{2}\mathds{H}}\;.

(F.18)

The even quadratic form is then defined as
(Q,Q)=2\det Q
and

\displaystyle p_{L}(Q)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\Bigl(\frac{m+q^{*}a+a^{*}q+|a|^{2}n}{R}+Rn\Bigr)\;,

\displaystyle p_{R}(Q)^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\Bigl(\frac{m+q^{*}a+a^{*}q+|a|^{2}n}{R}-Rn\Bigr)^{2}+2|q+an|^{2}\;.

(F.19)

One computes the Fourier expansion using the Rankin–Selberg unfolding method, the formula

\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{4\pi\sqrt{\tau_{2}x}}e^{-\frac{\pi}{\tau_{2}}x-\frac{\pi}{2}\tau_{2}x}\Bigl(\pi\tau_{2}xK_{\frac{s}{2}}(\tfrac{\pi\tau_{2}x}{2})+\bigl(\pi\tau_{2}x-s+1\bigr)K_{\frac{s-2}{2}}(\tfrac{\pi\tau_{2}x}{2})\Bigr)=K_{s-2}(2\pi x)\;,

(F.20)

and the property that the regularised integral of the product of two
SL(2,\mathds{Z})
Eisenstein series vanishes
[
100
]
. Recall that
\int_{\mathcal{F}_{2}}\frac{d^{2}\tau}{\tau_{2}^{\,2}}
must be regularised to take into account the split of the one-loop string amplitude in the sum of the Wilsonian effective action and the non-analytic component of the amplitude.

For
{\rm Re}[s]>4
one can unfold the domain
\mathcal{F}_{2}
using the Poincaré sum expression for
E_{\frac{s-3}{2},2}(\tau)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{F}_{2}}\frac{d^{2}\tau}{\tau_{2}^{\,2}}E_{\frac{s-3}{2},2}(\tau)\Gamma_{I\hskip-2.84526ptI_{1,1}[2]\oplus D_{4}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\pi^{-\frac{s}{2}}\Gamma(\tfrac{s}{2})\Biggl(\sum^{\prime}_{\begin{subarray}{c}Q\in I\hskip-2.84526ptI_{1,1}[2]\oplus D_{4}\\
Q^{2}=0\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{(2p_{R}(Q)^{2})^{\frac{s}{2}}}-\frac{1}{2}\hskip-8.53581pt\sum^{\prime}_{\begin{subarray}{c}Q\in I\hskip-2.84526ptI_{1,1}[\frac{1}{2}]\oplus D^{*}_{4}\\
Q^{2}=0\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{(2p_{R}(Q)^{2})^{\frac{s}{2}}}\Biggr)

(F.21)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\pi^{-\frac{s}{2}}\Gamma(\tfrac{s}{2})(1-2^{s-1})\sum^{\prime}_{\begin{subarray}{c}Q\in\mathds{M}^{2}_{2}\\
\det Q=0\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{\bigl({\rm tr}(v^{-1\dagger}Qv^{-1})^{2}\bigr)^{\frac{s}{2}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(2-2^{s})\xi(s)\frac{1}{\zeta_{\mathds{H}}(s)}\sum^{\prime}_{{\bf q}\in\alpha\mathds{H}\oplus\mathds{H}}\frac{1}{|v^{-1\dagger}({\bf q})|^{2s}}\;.

In this last step we use the property that any
Q\in\mathds{M}_{2}^{1}
with
\det Q=0
belongs to
\mathds{M}_{2}^{2}
, and any such
Q
factorises into
Q={\bf q}{\bf q}^{\dagger}
, with
{\bf q}
of right greatest common divisor over
\mathds{H}
a Hurwitz quaternion gcrd
({\bf q})
of norm square
|{\rm gcrd}({\bf q})|^{2}={\rm gcd}(Q)
, see equation (
F.13
).
Applying the same steps one computes that

\int_{\mathcal{F}_{2}}\frac{d^{2}\tau}{\tau_{2}^{\,2}}E_{\frac{s-3}{2},2}(2\tau)\Gamma_{I\hskip-2.84526ptI_{1,1}[2]\oplus D_{4}}=0\;,

(F.22)

for
{\rm Re}[s]>4
, and therefore for all
s
by analytic continuation. This is a consequence of the transformation property of the Siegel–Narain theta series under inversion
\tau\rightarrow-\frac{1}{\tau}
.

The lattice
I\hskip-2.84526ptI_{1,1}\oplus D_{4}
can be identified in the same way as
\mathds{M}_{2}^{0}
and one obtains that

\int_{\mathcal{F}_{2}}\frac{d^{2}\tau}{\tau_{2}^{\,2}}E_{\frac{s-3}{2},2}(2\tau)\Gamma_{I\hskip-2.84526ptI_{1,1}\oplus D_{4}}=2^{\frac{1-s}{2}}(2^{s-1}-1)\xi(s)E_{\mathds{H}^{2},s}^{\mathfrak{sl}_{2}\mathds{H}}\;.

(F.23)

while

\int_{\mathcal{F}_{2}}\frac{d^{2}\tau}{\tau_{2}^{\,2}}E_{\frac{s-3}{2},2}(\tau)\Gamma_{I\hskip-2.84526ptI_{1,1}\oplus D_{4}}=0\;.

(F.24)

This equation is true for any
SL(2,\mathds{Z})
modular form of weight two, and is ensures that the large twisted circle limit of the orbifold theory amplitude gives back the maximally supersymmetric amplitude.

One can also relate these Eisenstein series to Poincaré sums. An element
(q,p)\in\alpha\mathds{H}\oplus\mathds{H}
divided on the left by its left greatest common divisor
d\in\mathds{H}
is in the orbit of
(0,d)
if
d^{-1}q\in\alpha\mathds{H}
, and in the orbit of
(d,0)
if
d^{-1}q\notin\alpha\mathds{H}
. Using this one obtains that

\displaystyle E_{\alpha\mathds{H}\oplus\mathds{H},s}^{\mathfrak{sl}_{2}\mathds{H}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma^{\scalebox{0.6}{A}_{\scalebox{0.5}{3}}}_{0}(\alpha)/P_{1}}R^{s}|_{\gamma}+2^{-s}\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma^{\scalebox{0.6}{A}_{\scalebox{0.5}{3}}}_{0}(\alpha)/\bar{P}_{1}}\frac{R^{s}}{(R^{2}+|a|^{2})^{s}}\bigg|_{\gamma}\;,

\displaystyle E_{\mathds{H}^{2},s}^{\mathfrak{sl}_{2}\mathds{H}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma^{\scalebox{0.6}{A}_{\scalebox{0.5}{3}}}_{0}(\alpha)/P_{1}}R^{s}|_{\gamma}+\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma^{\scalebox{0.6}{A}_{\scalebox{0.5}{3}}}_{0}(\alpha)/\bar{P}_{1}}\frac{R^{s}}{(R^{2}+|a|^{2})^{s}}\bigg|_{\gamma}\ ,

(F.25)

where
R_{\scalebox{0.5}{T}}=\frac{R}{R^{2}+|a|^{2}}
is the T-dual radius and
P_{1}
(respectively
\bar{P}_{1}
) is the parabolic stabiliser of
(1,0)
(respectively
(0,1)
) in
\Gamma^{\scalebox{0.6}{A}_{\scalebox{0.5}{3}}}_{0}(\alpha)
.

F.3
\Gamma^{\scalebox{0.6}{D}_{\scalebox{0.5}{4}}}_{0}(\alpha)\subset Sp(4,\mathds{H})
as
O(I\hskip-2.84526ptI_{1,1}\oplus I\hskip-2.84526ptI_{1,1}[2]\oplus D_{4})

Before to define Eisenstein series, we will describe the three fundamental representations of
Spin(2,6)
and the corresponding discrete representations of the double cover of
Sp(4,\mathds{H})
. For short we will use the same notation
Sp(4,\mathds{H})
for the double cover.

One defines
g\in Sp(4,\mathds{H})
from the two by two matrices
A
,
B
,
C
and
D
over
\mathds{H}
such that

g=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\;A&\;B\\
\;C&\;D\end{array}\right)\ ,\qquad\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\;D^{\dagger}&-B^{\dagger}\\
-C^{\dagger}&\;A^{\dagger}\end{array}\right)\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\;A&\;B\\
\;C&\;D\end{array}\right)=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\;\mathds{1}&\;0\\
\;0&\;\mathds{1}\end{array}\right)\;.

(F.26)

A symplectic vector
{\bf k}
of
Sp(4,\mathds{H})
is a Weyl spinor of
Spin(2,6)
that can be defined as a real
SU(2)
doublet of Weyl spinors. We write the vector
{\bf k}\in\mathds{H}^{4}
as a doublet
{\bf k}=({\bf q},{\bf p})
of vector
{\bf q}
and
{\bf p}
in
\mathds{H}^{2}
, consistently with the decomposition of
g
in two by two matrices introduced above. The Weyl spinor representation admits the pure imaginary invariant bilinear form

{\bf k}^{\dagger}\omega{\bf k}={\bf q}^{\dagger}{\bf p}-{\bf p}^{\dagger}{\bf q}\in{\rm Im}[\mathds{H}]

(F.27)

that corresponds to the
SU(2)
triplet of
SO^{*}(8)
singlets obtained by applying the
SO^{*}(8)
bilinear form to the
SU(2)
doublet of vectors in the real representation
({\bf 2},{\bf 8})
of
SU(2)\times SO^{*}(8)
.

The negative chirality spinor
\widetilde{{\bf k}}
transforms in a representation of a trial
SO^{*}(8)\subset Spin(2,6)
. The algebraic relation between the two representations can only be written algebraically for the block diagonal matrix and the upper and lower triangular matrices separately, as

\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\;A&\;0\\
\;0&\;A^{-1\dagger}\end{array}\right)\rightarrow\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\;A^{-1\dagger}&\;0\\
\;0&\;A\end{array}\right)\;,\quad\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\;\mathds{1}&\;B\\
\;0&\;\mathds{1}\end{array}\right)\rightarrow\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\;\mathds{1}&\;\widetilde{B}\\
\;0&\;\mathds{1}\end{array}\right)\;,\quad\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\;\mathds{1}&\;0\\
\;C&\;\mathds{1}\end{array}\right)\rightarrow\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\;\mathds{1}&\;0\\
\;\widetilde{C}&\;\mathds{1}\end{array}\right)\;,

(F.28)

with
B
and
C
Hermitian and the tilde involution defined for
Q=\big(\scalebox{0.8}{$\begin{matrix}\tilde{m}&q\\
q^{*}&\tilde{n}\end{matrix}$}\big)
as

\widetilde{Q}\equiv\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\tilde{n}&-q\\
-q^{*}&\tilde{m}\end{array}\right)\;.

(F.29)

There is no algebraic relation for the general matrix
\big(\scalebox{0.8}{$\begin{matrix}A&B\\
C&D\end{matrix}$}\big)
because the two representations are defined for distinct double covers of the adjoint group
Sp(4,\mathds{H})/\mathds{Z}_{2}
. The tilde involution satisfies

Q\widetilde{Q}=\widetilde{Q}Q=\det Q\,\mathds{1}\ ,

(F.30)

and

Q\rightarrow A^{-1\dagger}QA^{-1}\;,\qquad\widetilde{Q}\rightarrow A\widetilde{Q}A^{\dagger}\;,

(F.31)

for
A\in SL(2,\mathds{H})
, which ensures that (
F.28
) is locally a homomorphism.

One defines the
SO(2,6)
vector
\mathcal{Q}
in

\mathds{F}^{0}_{2}=\mathds{Z}\oplus\mathds{M}_{2}^{0}\oplus\mathds{Z}=I\hskip-2.84526ptI_{2,2}\oplus D_{4}

(F.32)

with
Q\in\mathds{M}_{2}^{0}
transforming as above under
SL(2,\mathds{H})
and the two integers
m
,
n
, such that the unipotent elements act on
\mathcal{Q}=(n,Q,m)
as

(n,Q,m)\rightarrow\bigl(n,Q+\widetilde{B}n,m-{\rm tr}BQ-\det Bn\bigr)\;,\quad(n,Q,m)\rightarrow\bigl(n+{\rm tr}C\widetilde{Q}-\det Cm,Q-Cm,m\bigr)\;.

(F.33)

This action is consistent with the action of Gamma matrices acting on Dirac spinors
({\bf k},\widetilde{{\bf k}})
as the matrix

\Gamma(\mathcal{Q})=\left(\begin{array}[]{cccc}0&\,0&\,\widetilde{Q}&\,m\\
0&\,0&\,n&-Q\\
Q&\,m&\,0&\,0\\
n&-\widetilde{Q}&\,0&\,0\end{array}\right)\;,

(F.34)

which satisfies

\bigl\{\Gamma(\mathcal{Q}),\Gamma(\mathcal{Q}^{\prime})\bigr\}=(\mathcal{Q},\mathcal{Q}^{\prime})\mathds{1}\;,

(F.35)

for the bilinear form

(\mathcal{Q},\mathcal{Q})=2(mn+\det Q)\;.

(F.36)

As proved in
[
51
]
, a null symplectic vector
{\bf k}\in\mathds{H}^{4}
satisfying
{\bf k}^{\dagger}\omega{\bf k}=0
is in the
Sp(4,\mathds{H})
orbit of a canonical symplectic vector
\gamma{\bf k}=({\rm gcrd}({\bf k}),0,0,0)
where
{\rm gcrd}({\bf k})
is the greatest right divisor of
{\bf k}
. For any null vector
\mathcal{Q}\in\mathds{F}^{0}_{2}
, there exists a null symplectic vector
{\bf k}
such that
\mathcal{Q}\cdot{\bf k}=0
. Using the constraint on
\gamma{\bf k}
one obtains that

\gamma\mathcal{Q}=\left(0,\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}0&\,0\\
0&\,n\end{array}\right),m\right)\;,

(F.37)

and using then the
\gamma{\bf k}
Levi stabiliser
SL(2,\mathds{Z})\subset Sp(2,\mathds{H})\ltimes(\mathds{H}^{2}\oplus\mathds{Z})

\left(\begin{array}[]{cccc}1&\,\;0&\,\;0&\;\,0\\
0&a&0&b\\
0&0&1&0\\
0&c&0&d\end{array}\right)\left(\begin{array}[]{cccc}1&\,\;-q^{*}&\,k+q^{*}p&\;-p^{*}\\
0&1&-p&0\\
0&0&1&0\\
0&0&q&1\end{array}\right)

(F.38)

on
\gamma\mathcal{Q}
one obtains that there exists
\gamma^{\prime}
such that

\gamma^{\prime}\mathcal{Q}=\left(0,\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}0&\,0\\
0&\,0\end{array}\right),{\rm gcd}(\mathcal{Q})\right)\;.

(F.39)

F.4
Sp(4,\mathds{H})
and

Eisenstein series

One can now describe Eisenstein series for
\mathds{F}_{2}^{k}
with
k=0,1,2,3
. We define the Eisenstein series as the convergent sum for Re
[s]>3

E^{\mathfrak{sp}_{4}\mathds{H}}_{\mathds{F}^{k}_{2},s}=\frac{1}{2\zeta(2s)}\sum^{\prime}_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathcal{Q}\in\mathds{F}^{k}_{2}\\
(\mathcal{Q},\mathcal{Q})=0\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{|V(\mathcal{Q})|^{2s}}\;,

(F.72)

and as meromorphic functions of
s
on
\mathds{C}
by analytic continuations with a pole at the real value
s=3
. They are normalised to
1
at
s=0
. The definition of
|V(\mathcal{Q})|^{2}
and the
I\hskip-2.84526ptI_{1,1}\oplus I\hskip-2.84526ptI_{1,1}[2]\oplus D_{4}
bilinear form are

\displaystyle|V(\mathcal{Q})|^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle R^{-2}(m-{\rm tr}BQ-\det Bn)^{2}+{\rm tr}(v^{-1\dagger}(Q+\widetilde{B})v^{-1})^{2}+R^{2}n^{2}

\displaystyle(\mathcal{Q},\mathcal{Q})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2mn+2\det Q\;,

(F.73)

where
R
is the circle radius,
v
parametrises the five-dimensional NS moduli (i.e. the twisted circle radius and the Wilson line along
T^{4}
), while
B
parametrises NS axions. We will also use the notations

(Q,Q)=2\det Q\;,\qquad G(Q,Q)={\rm tr}(v^{-1\dagger}Qv^{-1})^{2}\;.

(F.74)

These Eisenstein series are related to the Siegel–Narain theta lifts

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{F}_{2}}\frac{d^{2}\tau}{\tau_{2}^{\,2}}E_{s-2,2}(2\tau)\Gamma_{I\hskip-2.84526ptI_{1,1}\oplus I\hskip-2.84526ptI_{1,1}[2]\oplus D_{4}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2^{s-1}\xi(2s)E^{\mathfrak{sp}_{4}\mathds{H}}_{\mathds{F}^{1}_{2},s}-2^{s}\xi(2s)E^{\mathfrak{sp}_{4}\mathds{H}}_{\mathds{F}^{2}_{2},s}\;,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{F}_{2}}\frac{d^{2}\tau}{\tau_{2}^{\,2}}E_{s-2,2}(\tau)\Gamma_{I\hskip-2.84526ptI_{1,1}\oplus I\hskip-2.84526ptI_{1,1}[2]\oplus D_{4}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\xi(2s)E^{\mathfrak{sp}_{4}\mathds{H}}_{\mathds{F}^{1}_{2},s}-2^{2s}\xi(2s)E^{\mathfrak{sp}_{4}\mathds{H}}_{\mathds{F}^{2}_{2},s}\;,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{F}_{2}}\frac{d^{2}\tau}{\tau_{2}^{\,2}}E_{s-2,2}(2\tau)\Gamma_{I\hskip-2.84526ptI_{2,2}\oplus D_{4}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2^{s-1}\xi(2s)E^{\mathfrak{sp}_{4}\mathds{H}}_{\mathds{F}^{0}_{2},s}-2^{s+1}\xi(2s)E^{\mathfrak{sp}_{4}\mathds{H}}_{\mathds{F}^{3}_{2},s}\;,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{F}_{2}}\frac{d^{2}\tau}{\tau_{2}^{\,2}}E_{s-2,2}(\tau)\Gamma_{I\hskip-2.84526ptI_{2,2}\oplus D_{4}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\xi(2s)E^{\mathfrak{sp}_{4}\mathds{H}}_{\mathds{F}^{0}_{2},s}-2^{2s+1}\xi(2s)E^{\mathfrak{sp}_{4}\mathds{H}}_{\mathds{F}^{3}_{2},s}\;.

(F.75)

Using the Rankin–Selberg unfolding method one can compute the large radius limit of the Siegel–Narain theta lifts. One obtains in this way that the last function vanishes identically

\int_{\mathcal{F}_{2}}\frac{d^{2}\tau}{\tau_{2}^{\,2}}E_{s-2,2}(\tau)\Gamma_{I\hskip-2.84526ptI_{2,2}\oplus D_{4}}=0\;.

(F.76)

This is due to the property that any null vector in
\mathds{F}^{3}_{2}
must be in
2\mathds{F}^{0}_{2}
, so that

E^{\mathfrak{sp}_{4}\mathds{H}}_{\mathds{F}^{3}_{2},s}=2^{-2s}E^{\mathfrak{sp}_{4}\mathds{H}}_{\mathds{F}^{0}_{2},s}\;.

(F.77)

It will nonetheless be useful to define the sublattice
\mathds{F}^{3}_{2}
for the non-perturbative completion
[
72
]
.

For the lattice
\mathds{F}_{2}^{1}
one computes using Rankin–Selberg unfolding method that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{F}_{2}}\frac{d^{2}\tau}{\tau_{2}^{\,2}}E_{s,2}(2\tau)\Gamma_{I\hskip-2.84526ptI_{1,1}\oplus I\hskip-2.84526ptI_{1,1}[2]\oplus D_{4}}

(F.78)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(2^{s+1}-2^{-s-2})\xi(2s+4)R^{2s+4}+(2^{s-1}-2^{-s})\frac{s}{s+1}\frac{\xi(2s+1)\xi(2s-1)}{\xi(2s+2)}R^{2-2s}

\displaystyle-\frac{R^{s+\frac{5}{2}}}{2^{2s+1}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}Q\in I\hskip-2.84526ptI_{1,1}[\frac{1}{2}]\oplus D_{4}^{*}\\
Q^{2}=0\end{subarray}}e^{2\pi i(Q,B)}\hskip-11.38109pt\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}d^{-1}Q\in I\hskip-2.84526ptI_{1,1}[\frac{1}{2}]\oplus D_{4}^{*}\\
d=1\,{\rm mod}\,2\end{subarray}}\hskip-8.53581ptd^{2s+3}\hskip 5.69054pt\frac{K_{s+\frac{3}{2}}(2\pi R\sqrt{2p_{R}(Q)^{2}})}{\sqrt{2p_{R}(Q)^{2}}^{s+\frac{3}{2}}}

\displaystyle+\frac{2^{2s+1}s\xi(2s+1)}{(s+1)\xi(2s+2)}R^{\frac{3}{2}-s}\hskip-14.22636pt\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}Q\in I\hskip-2.84526ptI_{1,1}[\frac{1}{2}]\oplus D_{4}^{*}\\
Q^{2}=0\end{subarray}}e^{2\pi i(Q,B)}\hskip-11.38109pt\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}d^{-1}Q\in I\hskip-2.84526ptI_{1,1}[\frac{1}{2}]\oplus D_{4}^{*}\\
d=1\,{\rm mod}\,2\end{subarray}}\hskip-8.53581ptd^{1-2s}\hskip 5.69054pt\frac{K_{s-\frac{1}{2}}(2\pi R\sqrt{2p_{R}(Q)^{2}})}{\sqrt{2p_{R}(Q)^{2}}^{\frac{1}{2}-s}}

\displaystyle-\frac{R}{2\xi(2s+2)}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}Q\in I\hskip-2.84526ptI_{1,1}[\frac{1}{2}]\oplus D_{4}^{*}\\
Q^{2}\neq 0\end{subarray}}\hskip-5.69054pte^{2\pi i(Q,B)}\hskip-19.91692pt\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}d^{-1}Q\in I\hskip-2.84526ptI_{1,1}[\frac{1}{2}]\oplus D_{4}^{*}\\
d=1\,{\rm mod}\,2\end{subarray}}\hskip-17.07164pt\frac{d^{2s+3}\sigma_{2s+1}(|\frac{Q^{2}}{d^{2}}|)}{|Q^{2}|^{s}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\hskip-8.53581ptdt\sqrt{t}W_{s,2}(\pi Q^{2}t)e^{-\frac{\pi}{t}R^{2}-\pi tG(Q,Q)}

where

W_{s,2}(2\pi x)=\frac{1}{(s+1)\sqrt{|x|}}\Bigl((s+1-2\pi x)K_{s+\frac{1}{2}}(2\pi|x|)-2\pi|x|K_{s+\frac{3}{2}}(2\pi|x|)\Bigr)

(F.79)

is defined such that

E_{s,2}(\tau)=\tau_{2}^{s}-\frac{s}{s+1}\frac{\xi(2s+1)}{\xi(2s+2)}\tau_{2}^{-1-s}+\frac{2}{\xi(2s+2)}\sum_{n\in\mathds{Z}}^{\prime}\frac{\sigma_{2s+1}(|n|)}{|n|^{s}}W_{s,2}(2\pi n\tau_{2})e^{2\pi i\tau_{1}}\;.

(F.80)

The same computation for
E_{s,2}(\tau)
gives

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{F}_{2}}\frac{d^{2}\tau}{\tau_{2}^{\,2}}E_{s,2}(\tau)\Gamma_{I\hskip-2.84526ptI_{1,1}\oplus I\hskip-2.84526ptI_{1,1}[2]\oplus D_{4}}

(F.81)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\xi(2s+4)R^{2s+4}-\frac{s}{s+1}\frac{\xi(2s+1)\xi(2s-1)}{\xi(2s+2)}R^{2-2s}+R\int_{\mathcal{F}_{2}}\frac{d^{2}\tau}{\tau_{2}^{\,2}}E_{s,2}(\tau)\Gamma_{I\hskip-2.84526ptI_{1,1}[2]\oplus D_{4}}

\displaystyle+2^{s+1}R^{s+\frac{5}{2}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}Q\in I\hskip-2.84526ptI_{1,1}[\frac{1}{2}]\oplus D_{4}^{*}\\
Q^{2}=0\end{subarray}}e^{2\pi i(Q,B)}\hskip-11.38109pt\sum_{d^{-1}Q\in I\hskip-2.84526ptI_{1,1}[\frac{1}{2}]\oplus D_{4}^{*}}\hskip-8.53581pt(-1)^{\frac{2n}{d}}d^{2s+3}\hskip 5.69054pt\frac{K_{s+\frac{3}{2}}(2\pi R\sqrt{2p_{R}(Q)^{2}})}{\sqrt{2p_{R}(Q)^{2}}^{s+\frac{3}{2}}}

\displaystyle-\frac{2^{s+1}s\xi(2s+1)}{(s+1)\xi(2s+2)}R^{\frac{3}{2}-s}\hskip-17.07164pt\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}Q\in I\hskip-2.84526ptI_{1,1}[\frac{1}{2}]\oplus D_{4}^{*}\\
Q^{2}=0\end{subarray}}e^{2\pi i(Q,B)}\hskip-17.07164pt\sum_{d^{-1}Q\in I\hskip-2.84526ptI_{1,1}[\frac{1}{2}]\oplus D_{4}^{*}}\hskip-14.22636pt(-1)^{\frac{2n}{d}}d^{1-2s}\hskip 5.69054pt\frac{K_{s-\frac{1}{2}}(2\pi R\sqrt{2p_{R}(Q)^{2}})}{\sqrt{2p_{R}(Q)^{2}}^{\frac{1}{2}-s}}

\displaystyle\hskip-11.38109pt+\frac{2^{s+1}R}{\xi(2s+2)}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}Q\in I\hskip-2.84526ptI_{1,1}[\frac{1}{2}]\oplus D_{4}^{*}\\
Q^{2}\neq 0\end{subarray}}\hskip-11.38109pte^{2\pi i(Q,B)}\hskip-25.60747pt\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}d^{-1}Q\in I\hskip-2.84526ptI_{1,1}[\frac{1}{2}]\oplus D_{4}^{*}\\
\frac{Q^{2}}{d^{2}}=0\,{\rm mod}\,2\end{subarray}}\hskip-22.76219pt\frac{(-1)^{\frac{2n}{d}}d^{2s+3}\sigma_{2s+1}(|\frac{Q^{2}}{2d^{2}}|)}{|Q^{2}|^{s}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\hskip-8.53581ptdt\sqrt{t}W_{s,2}(\pi Q^{2}t)e^{-\frac{\pi}{t}R^{2}-\pi tG(Q,Q)}

where
Q=\big(\scalebox{0.8}{$\begin{matrix}m&q\\
q^{*}&n\end{matrix}$}\big)
with

and
q\in\mathds{H}
and
2n=1
mod
2
if

.

One can use these formulae in the limit
s\rightarrow 0
to obtain the large radius expansion of the one-loop
\mathcal{R}^{4}
threshold function, but in practice it is more convenient to use directly the Rankin–Selberg unfolding method and the Fourier expansion of the holomorphic modular form
2{E}_{2}(2\tau)-{E}_{2}(\tau)
. One obtains

\displaystyle\frac{4\pi}{3}\int_{\mathcal{F}_{2}}\frac{d^{2}\tau}{\tau_{2}^{\,2}}\bigl(2{E}_{2}(2\tau)-{E}_{2}(\tau)\bigr)\Gamma_{I\hskip-2.84526ptI_{1,1}\oplus I\hskip-2.84526ptI_{1,1}[2]\oplus D_{4}}

(F.82)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\pi^{3}}{27}R^{4}-\frac{4\pi}{3}R\int_{\mathcal{F}_{2}}\frac{d^{2}\tau}{\tau_{2}^{\,2}}\hat{E}_{2}(\tau)\Gamma_{I\hskip-2.84526ptI_{1,1}[2]\oplus D_{4}}

\displaystyle+\frac{R}{3}\sum^{\prime}_{\begin{subarray}{c}Q\in I\hskip-2.84526ptI_{1,1}[\frac{1}{2}]\oplus D_{4}^{*}\\
Q^{2}=0\end{subarray}}\Biggl(\sum_{Q/d\in I\hskip-2.84526ptI_{1,1}[\frac{1}{2}]\oplus D_{4}^{*}}\hskip-14.22636ptd^{3}+4\hskip-8.53581pt\sum_{Q/d\in I\hskip-2.84526ptI_{1,1}[2]\oplus D_{4}}\hskip-14.22636ptd^{3}\,\Biggr)\frac{1+2\pi R\sqrt{2p_{R}(Q)^{2}}}{\sqrt{2p_{R}(Q)^{2}}^{3}}e^{-2\pi R\sqrt{2p_{R}(Q)^{2}}+2\pi i(Q,B)}

\displaystyle+8R\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}Q\in I\hskip-2.84526ptI_{1,1}[\frac{1}{2}]\oplus D_{4}^{*}\\
Q^{2}<0\end{subarray}}\Biggl(\sum_{Q/d\in I\hskip-2.84526ptI_{1,1}[\frac{1}{2}]\oplus D_{4}^{*}}\hskip-14.22636ptd^{3}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\ell|\frac{Q^{2}}{d^{2}}\\
\ell=1\,{\rm mod}\,2\end{subarray}}\hskip-8.53581pt\ell+4\hskip-8.53581pt\sum_{Q/d\in I\hskip-2.84526ptI_{1,1}[2]\oplus D_{4}}\hskip-14.22636ptd^{3}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\ell|\frac{Q^{2}}{2d^{2}}\\
\ell=1\,{\rm mod}\,2\end{subarray}}\hskip-8.53581pt\ell\,\Biggr)

\displaystyle\hskip 142.26378pt\times\frac{1+2\pi R\sqrt{2p_{R}(Q)^{2}}}{\sqrt{2p_{R}(Q)^{2}}^{3}}e^{-2\pi R\sqrt{2p_{R}(Q)^{2}}+2\pi i(Q,B)}

For the one-loop
{D}^{2}\mathcal{R}^{4}
threshold function we need the limit
s\rightarrow 1
. The limit diverges and the regularised limit gives

\displaystyle\lim_{s\rightarrow 1}\Bigl(\int_{\mathcal{F}_{2}}\frac{d^{2}\tau}{\tau_{2}^{\,2}}E_{s,2}(\tau)\Gamma_{I\hskip-2.84526ptI_{1,1}\oplus I\hskip-2.84526ptI_{1,1}[2]\oplus D_{4}}+\frac{s}{s+1}\frac{\xi(2s+1)\xi(2s-1)}{\xi(2s+2)}\Bigr)

(F.83)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{2\pi^{3}}{945}R^{6}+\frac{45\zeta(3)}{2\pi^{3}}\log R+R\int_{\mathcal{F}_{2}}\frac{d^{2}\tau}{\tau_{2}^{\,2}}E_{1,2}(\tau)\Gamma_{I\hskip-2.84526ptI_{1,1}[2]\oplus D_{4}}

\displaystyle+4R^{\frac{7}{2}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}Q\in I\hskip-2.84526ptI_{1,1}[\frac{1}{2}]\oplus D_{4}^{*}\\
Q^{2}=0\end{subarray}}e^{2\pi i(Q,B)}\hskip-11.38109pt\sum_{d^{-1}Q\in I\hskip-2.84526ptI_{1,1}[\frac{1}{2}]\oplus D_{4}^{*}}\hskip-8.53581pt(-1)^{\frac{2n}{d}}d^{5}\hskip 5.69054pt\frac{K_{\frac{5}{2}}(2\pi R\sqrt{2p_{R}(Q)^{2}})}{\sqrt{2p_{R}(Q)^{2}}^{\frac{5}{2}}}

\displaystyle\hskip-5.69054pt-\frac{45\zeta(3)}{\pi^{3}}\hskip-17.07164pt\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}Q\in I\hskip-2.84526ptI_{1,1}[\frac{1}{2}]\oplus D_{4}^{*}\\
Q^{2}=0\end{subarray}}\sum_{d^{-1}Q\in I\hskip-2.84526ptI_{1,1}[\frac{1}{2}]\oplus D_{4}^{*}}\hskip-14.22636pt(-1)^{\frac{2n}{d}}d^{-1}\hskip 5.69054pte^{-2\pi R\sqrt{2p_{R}(Q)^{2}}+2\pi i(Q,B)}

\displaystyle-\frac{180}{\pi^{3}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}Q\in I\hskip-2.84526ptI_{1,1}[\frac{1}{2}]\oplus D_{4}^{*}\\
Q^{2}>0\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}d^{-1}Q\in I\hskip-2.84526ptI_{1,1}[\frac{1}{2}]\oplus D_{4}^{*}\\
\frac{Q^{2}}{d^{2}}=0\,{\rm mod}\,2\end{subarray}}\hskip-22.76219pt\frac{(-1)^{\frac{2n}{d}}d^{5}\sigma_{3}(\frac{Q^{2}}{2d^{2}})}{Q^{6}}e^{-2\pi R\sqrt{2p_{L}(Q)^{2}}+2\pi i(Q,B)}

\displaystyle-\frac{180}{\pi^{3}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}Q\in I\hskip-2.84526ptI_{1,1}[\frac{1}{2}]\oplus D_{4}^{*}\\
Q^{2}<0\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}d^{-1}Q\in I\hskip-2.84526ptI_{1,1}[\frac{1}{2}]\oplus D_{4}^{*}\\
\frac{Q^{2}}{d^{2}}=0\,{\rm mod}\,2\end{subarray}}\hskip-22.76219pt\frac{(-1)^{\frac{2n}{d}}d^{5}\sigma_{3}(-\frac{Q^{2}}{2d^{2}})}{-Q^{6}}e^{-2\pi R\sqrt{2p_{R}(Q)^{2}}+2\pi i(Q,B)}

\displaystyle\hskip 85.35826pt\times\biggl(1-\frac{2\pi RQ^{2}}{\sqrt{2p_{R}(Q)^{2}}}\Bigl(1-\frac{Q^{2}}{4p_{R}(Q)^{2}}\Bigr)+\frac{\pi^{2}R^{2}Q^{4}}{p_{R}(Q)^{2}}\biggr)

As a final comment we will relate the Eisenstein series defined as constrained lattice sums to Poincaré sums associated to the three
\Gamma^{\scalebox{0.6}{D}_{\scalebox{0.5}{4}}}_{0}(\alpha)
orbit of null vectors in
\mathds{F}^{0}_{2}
and
\mathds{F}_{2}^{1}
. We denote them
\mathcal{O}_{1}
for the orbit of the momentum along the
S^{1}
,
\mathcal{O}_{2}
for the winding along the twisted
S^{1}
and
\mathcal{O}_{3}
for the momentum along the twisted
S^{1}
, and respectively
R
,
\tilde{R}_{\scalebox{0.5}{T}}
and
\tilde{R}
for the associated radius. These orbits are discrete cosets of
\Gamma^{\scalebox{0.6}{D}_{\scalebox{0.5}{4}}}_{0}(\alpha)

\mathcal{O}_{1}=\Gamma^{\scalebox{0.6}{D}_{\scalebox{0.5}{4}}}_{0}(\alpha)/(\Gamma^{\scalebox{0.6}{A}_{\scalebox{0.5}{3}}}_{0}(\alpha)\ltimes\mathds{M}_{2}^{1})\,,\;\mathcal{O}_{2}=\Gamma^{\scalebox{0.6}{D}_{\scalebox{0.5}{4}}}_{0}(\alpha)/(SL(2,\mathds{H})\ltimes\mathds{M}_{2}^{0})\,,\;\mathcal{O}_{3}=\Gamma^{\scalebox{0.6}{D}_{\scalebox{0.5}{4}}}_{0}(\alpha)/(SL(2,\mathds{H})\ltimes\mathds{M}^{3}_{2})\;,

(F.84)

by the corresponding stabilisers

\displaystyle\Gamma^{\scalebox{0.6}{A}_{\scalebox{0.5}{3}}}_{0}(\alpha)\ltimes\mathds{M}_{2}^{1}\;:\;g

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\begin{array}[]{cccc}a_{11}&\,\;a_{12}&\,\;b_{11}&\;\,b_{12}\\
a_{21}&a_{22}&b_{21}&b_{22}\\
0&0&d_{11}&d_{12}\\
0&0&d_{21}&d_{22}\end{array}\right)\;,

\displaystyle SL(2,\mathds{H})\ltimes\mathds{M}_{2}^{0}\;:\;g

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\begin{array}[]{cccc}a_{11}&\,\;b_{12}&\,\;b_{11}&\;\,a_{12}\\
0&d_{22}&d_{21}&0\\
0&d_{12}&d_{11}&0\\
a_{21}&b_{22}&b_{21}&a_{22}\end{array}\right)\;,

\displaystyle SL(2,\mathds{H})\ltimes\mathds{M}^{3}_{2}\;:\;g

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\begin{array}[]{cccc}d_{11}&\,\;0&\,\;0&\;\,d_{12}\\
b_{21}&a_{22}&a_{21}&b_{22}\\
b_{21}&a_{12}&a_{11}&b_{12}\\
d_{21}&0&0&d_{22}\end{array}\right)\;,

with
D=A^{-1\dagger}
and
A^{-1}B
Hermitian.
One has in this way

\displaystyle E^{\mathfrak{sp}_{4}\mathds{H}}_{\mathds{F}^{0}_{2},s}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\gamma\in\mathcal{O}_{1}}R^{2s}+\sum_{\gamma\in\mathcal{O}_{2}}\tilde{R}_{\scalebox{0.5}{T}}^{2s}+\sum_{\gamma\in\mathcal{O}_{3}}\tilde{R}^{2s}

\displaystyle E^{\mathfrak{sp}_{4}\mathds{H}}_{\mathds{F}^{1}_{2},s}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\gamma\in\mathcal{O}_{1}}R^{2s}+\sum_{\gamma\in\mathcal{O}_{2}}\tilde{R}_{\scalebox{0.5}{T}}^{2s}+2^{-2s}\sum_{\gamma\in\mathcal{O}_{3}}\tilde{R}^{2s}

\displaystyle E^{\mathfrak{sp}_{4}\mathds{H}}_{\mathds{F}^{2}_{2},s}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2^{-2s}\sum_{\gamma\in\mathcal{O}_{1}}R^{2s}+\sum_{\gamma\in\mathcal{O}_{2}}\tilde{R}_{\scalebox{0.5}{T}}^{2s}+2^{-2s}\sum_{\gamma\in\mathcal{O}_{3}}\tilde{R}^{2s}\;.

(F.100)

The relation can be used to define the Poincaré sums in function of the constrained lattice sums for almost all
s
. The limit
s\rightarrow 0
is singular, and the Poincaré sums are not constant at
s=0
. Instead, they depend individually on the regularised Eisenstein series at
s=3
. Nevertheless, the sum of the three Poincaré sums tends to one as
s\rightarrow 0
. More generally, for any three coefficients
c_{i}(s)
function of
s
with
c_{i}(s)=1+\mathcal{O}(s)
at
s=0
, one has

\lim_{s\rightarrow 0}\biggl(c_{1}(s)\sum_{\gamma\in\mathcal{O}_{1}}R^{2s}+c_{2}(s)\sum_{\gamma\in\mathcal{O}_{2}}\tilde{R}_{\scalebox{0.5}{T}}^{2s}+c_{3}(s)\sum_{\gamma\in\mathcal{O}_{3}}\tilde{R}^{2s}\biggr)=1\;.

(F.101)

Appendix G
Supergravity one-loop amplitude

The tree-level supergravity four-graviton amplitude is
46
46

46

We use the vielbein convention for
\kappa
commonly used in string theory amplitudes that is
\kappa=\frac{\kappa_{h}}{2}
with the metric
\kappa_{h}
used in field theory amplitudes
[
86
]
. Moreover we use the mostly plus signature for the Minkowski metric. We use otherwise the amplitude normalisation, that differs from the standard string theory normalisation by a factor of
4
.

\mathcal{M}^{\scalebox{0.6}{tree}}_{\scalebox{0.6}{sugra}}(1^{-},2^{-},3^{+},4^{+})=-i\kappa^{2}\langle 12\rangle^{4}[34]^{4}\frac{1}{stu}=-i\frac{\kappa^{2}}{2^{10}}t_{8}t_{8}\mathcal{R}^{4}\frac{64}{stu}\;.

(G.1)

It is the low energy limit of the string theory amplitude

\mathcal{M}^{\scalebox{0.6}{tree}}_{\scalebox{0.6}{type II}}(1^{-},2^{-},3^{+},4^{+})=\frac{i}{2^{11}}\frac{(2\pi)^{7}\alpha^{\prime 7}g_{\scalebox{0.5}{D}}^{\,2}}{(2\pi\sqrt{\alpha^{\prime}})^{10-D}}t_{8}t_{8}\mathcal{R}^{4}\frac{\Gamma(-\frac{\alpha^{\prime}s}{4})\Gamma(-\frac{\alpha^{\prime}t}{4})\Gamma(-\frac{\alpha^{\prime}u}{4})}{\Gamma(1+\frac{\alpha^{\prime}s}{4})\Gamma(1+\frac{\alpha^{\prime}t}{4})\Gamma(1+\frac{\alpha^{\prime}u}{4})}

(G.2)

with the identification
[
17
]

\frac{1}{2}\alpha^{\prime\frac{D-2}{2}}(2\pi)^{D-3}g_{\scalebox{0.5}{D}}^{\,2}=\kappa^{2}\;,

(G.3)

in
D
dimensions.

The one-loop supergravity
\mathcal{N}=6
amplitude in
D=4-2\epsilon
dimensions takes the form
[
86
]

\mathcal{M}^{\scalebox{0.6}{1-loop}}_{\scalebox{0.6}{sugra}}(1^{-},2^{-},3^{+},4^{+})=-i\kappa^{4}\langle 12\rangle^{4}[34]^{4}\Biggl(I_{4}(s,t)+I_{4}(t,u)+I_{4}(u,s)\\
-\frac{1}{s^{2}}\frac{D-2}{D-3}\Bigl(\frac{tu}{2}I_{4}(t,u)+tI_{3}(t)+uI_{3}(u)\Bigr)\\
+\frac{1}{su}\frac{D-4}{D-3}\Bigl(\frac{st}{2}I_{4}(s,t)+sI_{3}(s)+tI_{3}(t)\Bigr)+\frac{1}{st}\frac{D-4}{D-3}\Bigl(\frac{su}{2}I_{4}(s,u)+sI_{3}(s)+uI_{3}(u)\Bigr)\Biggr)

(G.4)

where

\displaystyle I_{4}(s,t)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int\frac{d^{D}p}{(2\pi)^{D}}\frac{1}{p^{2}(p-k_{1})^{2}(p-k_{1}-k_{2})^{2}(p+k_{4})^{2}}

\displaystyle I_{3}(s)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int\frac{d^{D}p}{(2\pi)^{D}}\frac{1}{p^{2}(p-k_{1}-k_{2})^{2}(p+k_{4})^{2}}

(G.5)

are the integral over the Euclidean momentum
p
. The first line is the
\mathcal{N}=8
supergravity amplitude and the two others line remove the contribution from the two additional gravitini multiplets. In Schwinger parameter space one obtains after some integrations by part

\displaystyle\frac{-1}{u}\frac{1}{D-3}\Bigl(\frac{st}{2}I_{4}(s,t)+sI_{3}(s)+tI_{3}(t)\Bigr)

(G.6)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2^{4}(2\pi)^{D-4}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\hskip-8.53581ptd\tau_{2}\,\tau_{2}^{\,3-\frac{D}{2}}\int_{0}^{1}\hskip-5.69054ptdx_{3}\int_{0}^{x_{3}}\hskip-5.69054ptdx_{2}\int_{0}^{x_{2}}\hskip-5.69054ptdx_{1}\,e^{\pi\tau_{2}[(x_{2}-x_{1})(1-x_{3})s+x_{1}(x_{3}-x_{2})t]}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\Biggl(\frac{1}{D-3}\frac{st}{s+t}\bigl(\tfrac{1}{2}-1+x_{3}-x_{1}\bigr)+\frac{2}{D-6}\bigl((x_{2}-x_{1})(1-x_{3})s+x_{1}(x_{3}-x_{2})t\bigr)\Biggr)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2^{4}(2\pi)^{D-4}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\hskip-8.53581ptd\tau_{2}\,\tau_{2}^{\,3-\frac{D}{2}}\frac{1}{\pi\tau_{2}}\int_{0}^{1}\hskip-5.69054ptdx_{3}\int_{0}^{x_{3}}\hskip-5.69054ptdx_{2}\int_{0}^{x_{2}}\hskip-5.69054ptdx_{1}\,e^{\pi\tau_{2}[(x_{2}-x_{1})(1-x_{3})s+x_{1}(x_{3}-x_{2})t]}

where one recognises the box integral in
D+2=6-2\epsilon
dimensions as is used in
[
86
]
. After several integrations by part, one obtains the representation of the amplitude

\displaystyle i\mathcal{M}^{\scalebox{0.6}{1-loop}}_{\scalebox{0.6}{sugra}}(1^{-},2^{-},3^{+},4^{+})

(G.7)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\kappa^{4}}{2^{4}(2\pi)^{D-4}}\langle 12\rangle^{4}[34]^{4}\int_{0}^{\infty}\hskip-8.53581ptd\tau_{2}\,\tau_{2}^{\,3-\frac{D}{2}}\int_{0}^{1}\hskip-5.69054ptdx_{3}\int_{0}^{x_{3}}\hskip-5.69054ptdx_{2}\int_{0}^{x_{2}}\hskip-5.69054ptdx_{1}

\displaystyle\times\Biggl(\Bigl(1-\frac{(1-x_{1}+x_{2}-x_{3})(x_{1}-x_{2}+x_{3})}{2}\Bigr)e^{\pi\tau_{2}[(x_{2}-x_{1})(1-x_{3})t+x_{1}(x_{3}-x_{2})u]}

\displaystyle\hskip-11.38109pt+\Bigl(1-\frac{(1-x_{1}+x_{2}-x_{3})(x_{1}-x_{2}+x_{3})}{2}-2(x_{2}-x_{1})(1-x_{3})\Bigr)\Bigl(e^{\pi\tau_{2}[(x_{2}-x_{1})(1-x_{3})s+x_{1}(x_{3}-x_{2})t]}

\displaystyle\hskip 227.62204pt+e^{\pi\tau_{2}[(x_{2}-x_{1})(1-x_{3})s+x_{1}(x_{3}-x_{2})u]}\Bigr)\Biggr)

which is more appropriate for comparison with the string theory amplitude. In particular the dependence in the dimension
D
is only in the power of
(2\pi)^{2}\tau_{2}
, and this formula is also valid in
D=5-2\epsilon
for four-dimensional polarisations.

We will now find that this matches the one-loop string theory amplitude (
4.24
) at low energy, fixing in this way the normalisation. For this purpose we consider the tropical limit
[
101
]
, such that
z_{a}\sim\tau y_{a}
and
\tau\sim i\tau_{2}
with
\tau_{2}>\hskip-5.69054pt>1
and
0\leq y_{a}\leq 1
. Using (
C.31
), one computes the tropical limits
47
47

47

We have for
z=x+i\tau_{2}y

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}(z)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\pi\tau_{2}(\tfrac{1}{6}+y^{2})-\log\bigl(|e^{\pi\tau_{2}y-i\pi x}{-}e^{-\pi\tau_{2}y+i\pi x}|^{2}\bigr)+\mathcal{O}(e^{-2\pi\tau_{2}})\;,

\displaystyle\mathcal{Y}(z)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-8\pi^{2}\Bigl(\tfrac{1}{6}-y\frac{e^{\pi\tau_{2}y-i\pi x}{+}e^{-\pi\tau_{2}y+i\pi x}}{e^{\pi\tau_{2}y-i\pi x}{-}e^{-\pi\tau_{2}y+i\pi x}}+y^{2}\Bigr)+\mathcal{O}(e^{-2\pi\tau_{2}})\;.

(G.8)

\mathcal{G}(z_{a}-z_{b})\sim 2\pi\tau_{2}\bigl(\tfrac{1}{6}-|y_{a}-y_{b}|+(y_{a}-y_{b})^{2}\bigr)\;,\quad\mathcal{Y}(z_{a}-z_{b})\sim-8\pi^{2}\bigl(\tfrac{1}{6}-|y_{a}-y_{b}|+(y_{a}-y_{b})^{2}\bigr)\;.

(G.9)

The tropical limit is obtained up to exponentially suppressed term in
e^{-\pi\tau_{2}}
at
y_{a}\neq y_{b}
.
Using then (
4.29
) and (
4.30
) one shows that

\displaystyle\sum_{\gamma\in PSL(2,\mathds{Z})/\Gamma_{0}(K)}(2\sin\!\tfrac{\pi}{K})^{2}\wp(\tfrac{1}{K})\Gamma_{I\hskip-2.84526ptI_{1,1}\oplus I\hskip-2.84526ptI_{1,1}[K]\oplus\mathds{H}(K)}

(G.10)

\displaystyle\underset{K=2}{\sim}

\displaystyle\frac{2\pi^{2}}{3}\tau_{2}^{3}\Biggl(5\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathcal{Q}\in I\hskip-2.84526ptI_{1,1}\oplus I\hskip-2.84526ptI_{1,1}[2]\oplus\mathds{H}\\
\mathcal{Q}^{2}=0\end{subarray}}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathcal{Q}\in I\hskip-2.84526ptI_{1,1}\oplus I\hskip-2.84526ptI_{1,1}[\frac{1}{2}]\oplus\frac{1}{\alpha}\mathds{H}\smallsetminus I\hskip-2.84526ptI_{1,1}\oplus I\hskip-2.84526ptI_{1,1}[2]\oplus\mathds{H}\\
\mathcal{Q}^{2}=0\end{subarray}}\Biggr)e^{-4\pi\tau_{2}p_{R}(\mathcal{Q})^{2}}

\displaystyle\underset{K=3}{\sim}

\displaystyle\frac{2\pi^{2}}{3}\tau_{2}^{3}\Biggl(5\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathcal{Q}\in I\hskip-2.84526ptI_{1,1}\oplus I\hskip-2.84526ptI_{1,1}[3]\oplus\mathds{H}(3)\\
\mathcal{Q}^{2}=0\end{subarray}}+\hskip 22.76219pt\tfrac{1}{2}\hskip-28.45274pt\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathcal{Q}\in I\hskip-2.84526ptI_{1,1}\oplus I\hskip-2.84526ptI_{1,1}[\frac{1}{3}]\oplus\frac{1}{\alpha}\mathds{H}(3)\smallsetminus I\hskip-2.84526ptI_{1,1}\oplus I\hskip-2.84526ptI_{1,1}[3]\oplus\mathds{H}(3)\\
\mathcal{Q}^{2}=0\end{subarray}}\Biggr)e^{-4\pi\tau_{2}p_{R}(\mathcal{Q})^{2}}

and

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\gamma\in PSL(2,\mathds{Z})/\Gamma_{0}(K)}(2\sin\!\tfrac{\pi}{K})^{2}\Gamma_{I\hskip-2.84526ptI_{1,1}\oplus I\hskip-2.84526ptI_{1,1}[K]\oplus\mathds{H}(K)}

(G.11)

\displaystyle\underset{K=2}{\sim}

\displaystyle\tau_{2}^{3}\Biggl(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathcal{Q}\in I\hskip-2.84526ptI_{1,1}\oplus I\hskip-2.84526ptI_{1,1}[2]\oplus\mathds{H}\\
\mathcal{Q}^{2}=0\end{subarray}}-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathcal{Q}\in I\hskip-2.84526ptI_{1,1}\oplus I\hskip-2.84526ptI_{1,1}[\frac{1}{2}]\oplus\frac{1}{\alpha}\mathds{H}\smallsetminus I\hskip-2.84526ptI_{1,1}\oplus I\hskip-2.84526ptI_{1,1}[2]\oplus\mathds{H}\\
\mathcal{Q}^{2}=0\end{subarray}}\Biggr)e^{-4\pi\tau_{2}p_{R}(\mathcal{Q})^{2}}

\displaystyle\underset{K=3}{\sim}

\displaystyle\tau_{2}^{3}\Biggl(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathcal{Q}\in I\hskip-2.84526ptI_{1,1}\oplus I\hskip-2.84526ptI_{1,1}[3]\oplus\mathds{H}(3)\\
\mathcal{Q}^{2}=0\end{subarray}}-\hskip 22.76219pt\tfrac{1}{2}\hskip-28.45274pt\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathcal{Q}\in I\hskip-2.84526ptI_{1,1}\oplus I\hskip-2.84526ptI_{1,1}[\frac{1}{3}]\oplus\frac{1}{\alpha}\mathds{H}(3)\smallsetminus I\hskip-2.84526ptI_{1,1}\oplus I\hskip-2.84526ptI_{1,1}[3]\oplus\mathds{H}(3)\\
\mathcal{Q}^{2}=0\end{subarray}}\Biggr)e^{-4\pi\tau_{2}p_{R}(\mathcal{Q})^{2}}\;,

which gives the supergravity limit, including the Kaluza–Klein tower of spin two and spin three-half supermultiplets. Keeping only the massless graviton multiplet with
\mathcal{Q}=0
, one gets

\displaystyle i\mathcal{M}^{\scalebox{0.6}{1-loop}}_{\scalebox{0.6}{type II}}(1^{-},2^{-},3^{+},4^{+})

(G.13)

\displaystyle\sim

\displaystyle\frac{\alpha^{\prime\,2+\frac{D}{2}}g_{\scalebox{0.5}{D}}^{\,4}(2\pi)^{D-4}}{2^{8}}\langle 12\rangle^{4}[34]^{4}\int_{0}^{\infty}\hskip-5.69054ptd\tau_{2}\tau_{2}^{3-\frac{D}{2}}\prod_{a=1}^{4}\int_{0}^{1}\hskip-5.69054ptdy_{a}\delta(y_{4})\;e^{-\frac{\alpha^{\prime}}{2}\sum_{a>b}\mathcal{G}(z_{a}-z_{b})k_{a}\cdot k_{b}}

\displaystyle\hskip-5.69054pt\times\Bigl(\frac{20\pi^{2}}{3}+\tfrac{1}{8}\bigl(\mathcal{Y}(z_{12})+\mathcal{Y}(z_{34})-\mathcal{Y}(z_{13})-\mathcal{Y}(z_{14})-\mathcal{Y}(z_{23})-\mathcal{Y}(z_{24})\bigr)\Bigr)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\alpha^{\prime\,2+\frac{D}{2}}g_{\scalebox{0.5}{D}}^{\,4}(2\pi)^{D-2}}{2^{6}}\langle 12\rangle^{4}[34]^{4}\int_{0}^{\infty}\hskip-8.53581ptd\tau_{2}\,\tau_{2}^{\,3-\frac{D}{2}}\int_{0}^{1}\hskip-5.69054ptdx_{3}\int_{0}^{x_{3}}\hskip-5.69054ptdx_{2}\int_{0}^{x_{2}}\hskip-5.69054ptdx_{1}

\displaystyle\times\Biggl(\Bigl(1-\frac{(1-x_{1}+x_{2}-x_{3})(x_{1}-x_{2}+x_{3})}{2}\Bigr)e^{\pi\tau_{2}\alpha^{\prime}[(x_{2}-x_{1})(1-x_{3})t+x_{1}(x_{3}-x_{2})u]}

\displaystyle\hskip-17.07164pt+\Bigl(1-\frac{(1-x_{1}+x_{2}-x_{3})(x_{1}-x_{2}+x_{3})}{2}-2(x_{2}-x_{1})(1-x_{3})\Bigr)\Bigl(e^{\pi\tau_{2}\alpha^{\prime}[(x_{2}-x_{1})(1-x_{3})s+x_{1}(x_{3}-x_{2})t]}

\displaystyle\hskip 221.93149pt+e^{\pi\tau_{2}\alpha^{\prime}[(x_{2}-x_{1})(1-x_{3})s+x_{1}(x_{3}-x_{2})u]}\Bigr)\Biggr)

consistently with the supergravity amplitude using (
G.3
). In the computation we have decomposed the integral over the three
y_{a}
variables into the 6 ordered integrals, that give two times the three integrals with the orders
0\leq y_{1}\leq y_{2}\leq y_{3}
,
0\leq y_{2}\leq y_{3}\leq y_{1}
and
0\leq y_{3}\leq y_{1}\leq y_{2}
. This gives in total two times the three corresponding integrals over
0\leq x_{1}\leq x_{2}\leq x_{3}\leq 1
with the three respective changes of variables

\displaystyle y_{1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 1-x_{3}\;,\qquad y_{2}=1-x_{2}\;,\qquad y_{3}=1-x_{1}\;,

(G.14)

\displaystyle y_{1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 1-x_{1}\;,\qquad y_{2}=1-x_{3}\;,\qquad y_{3}=1-x_{2}\;,

(G.15)

\displaystyle y_{1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle x_{2}\;,\qquad y_{2}=x_{3}\;,\qquad y_{3}=x_{1}\;.

(G.16)

Note as a cross check that the contribution from the one-half BPS states in (
G.10
) and (
G.11
) gives also the expected contribution from spin two and spin three-half supermultiplets in supergravity.

Appendix H
The Dabholkar–Harvey
\mathds{Z}_{3}
orbifold

In this appendix we discuss another string theory construction of
\mathcal{N}=6
supergravity introduced in
[
48
]
, which is defined as the asymmetric
\mathds{Z}_{3}
orbifold acting on
T^{6}
at the
A_{2}\oplus A_{2}\oplus A_{2}
symmetric point. The untwisted sector breaks supersymmetry down to
\mathcal{N}=5
, but the twisted sector includes one massless
\mathcal{N}=5
gravitino multiplet. We want to argue that this theory is identical to the
\mathds{Z}_{3}
orbifold theory introduced in Section
2
at the point in Narain moduli space where the left and right projections of the lattice
I\hskip-2.84526ptI_{1,1}\oplus I\hskip-2.84526ptI_{1,1}[3]\oplus A_{2}\oplus A_{2}
factorise into the left
A_{2}
lattice and the right
A_{2}\oplus A_{2}\oplus A_{2}
lattice, i.e. at zero Wilson lines and for the metric
G
and
B
field on
T^{2}

G=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}3&-\frac{3}{2}\\
-\frac{3}{2}&\;1\end{array}\right)\;,\qquad B=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}0&-\frac{3}{2}\\
\frac{3}{2}&\;0\end{array}\right)\;.

(H.1)

We will show that the spectrum of the two theories are identical. Using
[
58
]
, one obtains for the character valued partition function with the insertion of the
\mathds{Z}_{3}
generator
g

\frac{\vartheta_{3}(v)\vartheta_{3}(\frac{1}{3})^{2}\vartheta_{3}(-\frac{2}{3})-\sum_{\alpha=1,2,4}\vartheta_{\alpha}(v)\vartheta_{\alpha}(\frac{1}{3})^{2}\vartheta_{\alpha}(-\frac{2}{3})}{2\eta(\tau)^{3}\vartheta_{1}(\frac{1}{3})^{3}}=-\frac{\vartheta_{1}(\frac{v}{2})\vartheta_{1}(\frac{v}{2}-\frac{1}{3})^{3}}{\eta(\tau)^{3}\vartheta_{1}(\frac{1}{3})^{3}}\;,

(H.2)

and the same with
\frac{1}{3}
replaced by
-\frac{1}{3}
for the insertion of
g^{-1}
, such that the character valued partition function is

{\cal Z}^{\mathds{Z}_{3}}_{D=4}(v,\bar{v})=\frac{\xi(v)}{3}\overline{\frac{\xi({v})\vartheta_{1}(\frac{v}{2})^{4}}{\eta(\tau)^{12}}}\Biggl[\frac{{\vartheta_{1}({v\over 2})^{4}}}{\eta(\tau)^{12}}\Lambda_{I\hskip-2.84526ptI_{6,6}}+(2\sin\tfrac{\pi}{3})^{3}\frac{\vartheta_{1}(\frac{v}{2})\bigl(\vartheta_{1}(\frac{1}{3}-\frac{v}{2})^{3}-\vartheta_{1}(\frac{1}{3}+\frac{v}{2})^{3}\bigr)}{\eta(\tau)^{3}\vartheta_{1}(\frac{1}{3})^{3}}\overline{\Lambda}_{A_{2}}^{\;3}\\
-\frac{i}{\sqrt{27}}(2\sin\tfrac{\pi}{3})^{3}\sum_{r=0}^{2}\Bigl(\frac{\vartheta_{1}(\frac{v}{2})\bigl(\vartheta_{1}(\frac{\tau}{3}-\frac{v}{2})^{3}-\vartheta_{1}(\frac{\tau}{3}+\frac{v}{2})^{3}\bigr)}{\eta(\tau)^{3}\vartheta_{1}(\frac{\tau}{3})^{3}}\overline{\Lambda}_{A_{2}^{*}}^{\;3}\Bigr)\Big|_{\tau\rightarrow\tau+r}\Biggr]

(H.3)

where the last line is the contribution from the twisted sectors that is obtained by modular transformations. As in
2.3
, it is convenient to decompose the Lorentzian lattice partition function as a sum over orbits

\Lambda_{I\hskip-2.84526ptI_{6,6}}=\sum_{\gamma\in P\hskip-0.56905ptSL(2,\mathds{Z})/\Gamma_{0}(3)}\Bigl(\bigl(\Lambda_{A_{2}}^{\;3}-\tfrac{1}{8}(\Lambda_{A^{*}_{2}}-\Lambda_{A_{2}})^{3}\bigr)\overline{\Lambda}_{A_{2}}^{3}\Bigr)\Big|_{\gamma}

(H.4)

where the seed can be identified as

\Lambda_{A_{2}}^{\;3}-\tfrac{1}{8}(\Lambda_{A^{*}_{2}}-\Lambda_{A_{2}})^{3}=(2\sin\tfrac{\pi}{3})^{3}\frac{\eta(\tau)^{9}}{\vartheta_{1}(\frac{1}{3})^{3}}\;.

(H.5)

With the help of this formula one can rewrite the character valued partition function as

{\cal Z}^{\mathds{Z}_{3}}_{D=4}(v,\bar{v})=\sqrt{3}\,\overline{\frac{\xi({v})\vartheta_{1}(\frac{v}{2})^{4}}{\eta(\tau)^{12}}}\Biggl[\frac{\xi(v)\vartheta_{1}(\frac{v}{2})\bigl(\vartheta_{1}(\frac{v}{2})^{3}+\vartheta_{1}(\frac{1}{3}-\frac{v}{2})^{3}-\vartheta_{1}(\frac{1}{3}+\frac{v}{2})^{3}\bigr)}{\eta(\tau)^{3}\vartheta_{1}(\frac{1}{3})^{3}}\overline{\Lambda}_{A_{2}}^{\;3}\\
-\frac{i}{\sqrt{27}}\sum_{r=0}^{2}\Bigl(\frac{\xi(v)\vartheta_{1}(\frac{v}{2})\bigl(\vartheta_{1}(\frac{v}{2})^{3}+\vartheta_{1}(\frac{\tau}{3}-\frac{v}{2})^{3}-\vartheta_{1}(\frac{\tau}{3}+\frac{v}{2})^{3}\bigr)}{\eta(\tau)^{3}\vartheta_{1}(\frac{\tau}{3})^{3}}\overline{\Lambda}_{A_{2}^{*}}^{\;3}\Bigr)\Big|_{\tau\rightarrow\tau+r}\Biggr]\;.

(H.6)

Using then the theta function identity

\vartheta_{1}^{\prime}(0)\Bigl(\vartheta_{1}(z)^{3}-\vartheta_{1}(z+\tfrac{1}{3})^{3}-\vartheta_{1}(z-\tfrac{1}{3})^{3}\Bigr)=6\vartheta_{1}^{\prime}(\tfrac{1}{3})\vartheta_{1}(z)\vartheta_{1}(z+\tfrac{1}{3})\vartheta_{1}(z-\tfrac{1}{3})\;,

(H.7)

and

\frac{\sqrt{3}}{\pi}\frac{\vartheta_{1}^{\prime}(\frac{1}{3})}{\vartheta_{1}(\frac{1}{3})}=\Lambda_{A_{2}}\;,

(H.8)

one finds that

\sqrt{3}\,\frac{\vartheta_{1}(\frac{v}{2})\bigl(\vartheta_{1}(\frac{v}{2})^{3}+\vartheta_{1}(\frac{1}{3}-\frac{v}{2})^{3}-\vartheta_{1}(\frac{1}{3}+\frac{v}{2})^{3}\bigr)}{\eta(\tau)^{3}\vartheta_{1}(\frac{1}{3})^{3}}=3\frac{\vartheta_{1}(\frac{v}{2})^{2}\vartheta_{1}(\frac{v}{2}+\frac{1}{3})\vartheta_{1}(\frac{v}{2}-\frac{1}{3})}{\eta(\tau)^{6}\vartheta_{1}(\frac{1}{3})^{2}}\Lambda_{A_{2}}\;.

(H.9)

Because the twisted sector is obtained by modular transformations, we conclude that

\displaystyle Z^{\mathds{Z}_{3}}_{D=4}(y)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 3\int_{-\frac{1}{2}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\hskip-7.11317ptd\tau_{1}\overline{\frac{\xi({v})\vartheta_{1}(\frac{v}{2})^{4}}{\eta(\tau)^{12}}}\frac{\xi(v){\vartheta_{1}(\frac{v}{2})^{2}\vartheta_{1}(\frac{v}{2}{+}\frac{1}{3})\vartheta_{1}(\frac{v}{2}{-}\frac{1}{3})}}{\vartheta_{1}(\frac{1}{3})^{2}\eta(\tau)^{6}}\Lambda_{A_{2}}\overline{\Lambda}_{A_{2}}^{\;3}

(H.10)

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt-\frac{1}{3}\int_{-\frac{3}{2}}^{\frac{3}{2}}\hskip-5.69054ptd\tau_{1}\overline{\frac{\xi({v})\vartheta_{1}(\frac{v}{2})^{4}}{\eta(\tau)^{12}}}\frac{\xi(v){\vartheta_{1}(\frac{v}{2})^{2}\vartheta_{1}(\frac{v}{2}{+}\frac{\tau}{3})\vartheta_{1}(\frac{v}{2}{-}\frac{\tau}{3})}}{\vartheta_{1}(\frac{\tau}{3})^{2}\eta(\tau)^{6}}\Lambda_{A_{2}^{*}}\overline{\Lambda}_{A_{2}^{*}}^{\;3}\;,

as in (
2.37
) at the point (
H.1
) in Narain moduli space where

\Lambda_{I\hskip-2.84526ptI_{1,1}\oplus I\hskip-2.84526ptI_{1,1}[3]\oplus\mathds{H}(3)}=\Lambda_{A_{2}}\overline{\Lambda}_{A_{2}}^{\;3}\;.

(H.11)

This proves that the two theories have the same perturbative spectrum, which provides strong evidence that the Dabholkar–Harvey
\mathds{Z}_{3}
orbifold theory is just a particular example of the
\mathds{Z}_{3}
orbifold theory analysed in this paper.
